<a id='question-114'></a>

### Question 114: Cell-Mediated Immunity: Part 3 Focus 14 (Memory)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the role of memory T cells (specifically CD8+ cytotoxic T lymphocytes, CTLs) in secondary immune responses, particularly in the context of graft rejection and the mechanisms by which they recognize and eliminate target cells. (理解记忆T细胞（特别是CD8+细胞毒性T淋巴细胞，CTLs）在继发免疫应答中的作用，特别是在移植物排斥的背景下，以及它们识别和清除靶细胞的机制。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with a history of chronic kidney disease receives a kidney transplant from a deceased donor with a mismatched HLA-A allele. He is initially treated with immunosuppressive therapy including tacrolimus and mycophenolate mofetil. Six months post-transplant, despite seemingly stable renal function (creatinine 1.5 mg/dL), he develops a severe, rapidly progressive interstitial nephritis characterized by diffuse tubular cell damage and infiltration of mononuclear cells. Renal biopsy reveals extensive infiltration of CD8+ T lymphocytes expressing granzyme B and perforin within the tubular epithelium. Flow cytometry analysis of peripheral blood lymphocytes shows a significantly increased population of CD8+ T cells specific for the donor HLA-A allele, exhibiting enhanced proliferation and cytokine (IFN-γ) production upon stimulation with donor antigen-presenting cells. These donor-specific CD8+ T cells recognize peptides derived from the mismatched HLA-A allele presented on the surface of recipient tubular epithelial cells.

Which of the following cellular mechanisms is MOST directly responsible for the observed graft damage in this patient?

(A) Antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) mediated by NK cells recognizing antibody-coated graft cells.
(B) Complement-dependent cytotoxicity (CDC) mediated by antibodies binding to graft cell surface antigens.
(C) Direct cytotoxicity mediated by donor-specific CD8+ T lymphocytes recognizing alloantigens presented on MHC class I molecules of recipient cells.
(D) Type III hypersensitivity reaction involving the deposition of immune complexes in the glomeruli.
(E) Type IV hypersensitivity reaction mediated by Th2 cells and eosinophils leading to mast cell degranulation.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male, kidney transplant recipient. This sets the stage for potential graft rejection.
- **Transplant Details:** Deceased donor, mismatched HLA-A allele. HLA mismatch is a major trigger for T-cell mediated rejection.
- **Immunosuppression:** Tacrolimus (calcineurin inhibitor, inhibits T-cell activation) and mycophenolate mofetil (inhibits lymphocyte proliferation). Despite this, rejection occurs, suggesting breakthrough or insufficient suppression.
- **Clinical Presentation:** Severe, rapidly progressive interstitial nephritis 6 months post-transplant. This timing and location are characteristic of acute cellular rejection.
- **Histopathology:** Diffuse tubular cell damage, mononuclear cell infiltration, CD8+ T lymphocytes expressing granzyme B and perforin within tubular epithelium. This strongly points to cell-mediated cytotoxicity targeting tubular cells. Granzyme B and perforin are key effector molecules of CTLs.
- **Immunological Findings:** Increased population of donor-specific CD8+ T cells in peripheral blood, enhanced proliferation and IFN-γ production upon stimulation with donor antigen-presenting cells. This confirms the presence of a robust, specific T-cell response against the donor graft.
- **Mechanism of Recognition:** Donor-specific CD8+ T cells recognize peptides from the mismatched HLA-A allele presented on MHC class I molecules of recipient tubular epithelial cells. This is the hallmark of direct allorecognition by CTLs.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the MOST direct cellular mechanism causing graft damage.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Graft Rejection:** This is a classic example of an immune response against foreign tissue (alloantigens). The primary alloantigens are MHC molecules (HLA in humans).
- **Cell-Mediated Immunity (CMI):** Rejection is primarily mediated by T lymphocytes.
- **CD8+ T Lymphocytes (CTLs):** These are the key effector cells in CMI against intracellular pathogens and altered self-cells (like virally infected or tumor cells). In graft rejection, they directly recognize and kill foreign cells expressing alloantigens.
- **MHC Class I Presentation:** All nucleated cells express MHC class I molecules. They present endogenous peptides (including those derived from foreign proteins like donor HLA) to CD8+ T cells.
- **T Cell Receptor (TCR) Recognition:** The TCR on a CD8+ T cell specifically recognizes a peptide-MHC class I complex. If the peptide is derived from a foreign MHC allele (like the mismatched HLA-A), the T cell becomes activated.
- **CTL Effector Mechanisms:** Activated CTLs kill target cells primarily through two mechanisms:
    - **Perforin/Granzyme Pathway:** CTLs release perforin, which forms pores in the target cell membrane, and granzymes, which enter through these pores and activate caspases, leading to apoptosis.
    - **Fas/FasL Pathway:** CTLs express Fas ligand (FasL), which binds to Fas (CD95) on target cells, triggering apoptosis.
- **Memory T Cells:** The patient is 6 months post-transplant. The initial sensitization occurred shortly after transplant. The observed enhanced proliferation and cytokine production indicate a secondary (memory) T-cell response, which is typically faster and stronger than the primary response. Memory CD8+ T cells are crucial for long-term graft surveillance and rejection.
- **Type IV Hypersensitivity:** Cellular rejection is a form of Type IV hypersensitivity, characterized by T-cell mediated inflammation and tissue damage.

##### 3. Specifics of the Case:**
- The biopsy shows CD8+ T cells infiltrating the tubular epithelium, expressing granzyme B and perforin. This confirms direct CTL-mediated cytotoxicity.
- The peripheral blood analysis shows donor-specific CD8+ T cells recognizing the mismatched HLA-A allele presented on MHC class I. This confirms the specificity and mechanism of recognition.
- The clinical picture (rapidly progressive interstitial nephritis) is consistent with acute cellular rejection mediated by CTLs.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) mediated by NK cells recognizing antibody-coated graft cells.** ADCC involves antibodies binding to target cells, which are then recognized and killed by effector cells (like NK cells) expressing Fc receptors. While antibodies can play a role in rejection (especially chronic rejection or antibody-mediated rejection), the primary mechanism described here (CD8+ T cells, granzyme B/perforin, direct recognition of MHC I) is CTL-mediated cytotoxicity, not ADCC. NK cells typically recognize cells lacking MHC class I or expressing stress ligands, not antibody-coated cells in this context.
- (B) **Complement-dependent cytotoxicity (CDC) mediated by antibodies binding to graft cell surface antigens.** CDC involves antibodies activating the complement cascade, leading to the formation of the membrane attack complex (MAC) and lysis of target cells. While antibodies can be involved in rejection, the vignette specifically highlights CD8+ T cells and their cytotoxic molecules (granzyme B, perforin), which are characteristic of CTL-mediated killing, not CDC.
- (D) **Type III hypersensitivity reaction involving the deposition of immune complexes in the glomeruli.** Type III hypersensitivity involves the formation and deposition of antigen-antibody immune complexes, leading to complement activation and inflammation, often in blood vessels or glomeruli. This typically presents with vasculitis or glomerulonephritis, not primarily interstitial nephritis with tubular cell infiltration by CTLs. The vignette describes cellular infiltration and direct cell killing, not immune complex deposition.
- (E) **Type IV hypersensitivity reaction mediated by Th2 cells and eosinophils leading to mast cell degranulation.** Type IV hypersensitivity is T-cell mediated, but this option describes a specific subset mediated by Th2 cells, eosinophils, and mast cells. This mechanism is typically associated with allergic reactions or parasitic infections, not acute cellular graft rejection. Acute cellular rejection is primarily mediated by Th1 cells (producing IFN-γ) and CD8+ CTLs.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Correct Answer (C) - CTL-mediated Rejection | Option (A) - ADCC (NK cells) | Option (B) - CDC (Antibodies/Complement) | Option (D) - Type III (Immune Complexes) | Option (E) - Th2/Eosinophil Hypersensitivity |
|--------------------------|---------------------------------------------|-----------------------------|------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------|
| **Primary Effector Cell** | CD8+ T Lymphocytes (CTLs)                   | NK cells                    | Complement system                        | Neutrophils, Macrophages                  | Eosinophils, Mast Cells                     |
| **Recognition Mechanism** | TCR recognizing peptide-MHC I                | Fc receptors (NK cells)     | Antibody binding to antigen               | Immune complex deposition                 | IgE binding to mast cells/eosinophils      |
| **Key Molecules**        | Perforin, Granzyme B, FasL                  | Fc receptors                | Antibodies, C3b, MAC                      | Immune complexes, C3a, C5a                | IgE, Histamine, Eosinophil granule proteins |
| **Target Cell Killing**   | Apoptosis (Perforin/Granzyme, FasL)         | Apoptosis (via Fc receptors)| Lysis (MAC)                               | Inflammation, Tissue Damage               | Degranulation, Inflammation                |
| **Clinical Context**      | Acute Cellular Graft Rejection              | Some viral infections, tumors| Antibody-mediated rejection, some infections| Vasculitis, Glomerulonephritis           | Allergy, Parasitic Infections              |
| **Vignette Clues**        | CD8+ T cells, Granzyme B, Perforin, MHC I   | None                       | None                                       | None                                       | None                                         |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The primary mechanism of acute cellular graft rejection is direct cytotoxicity mediated by recipient CD8+ T lymphocytes recognizing donor alloantigens (like mismatched HLA) presented on MHC class I molecules of graft cells. This process involves the release of perforin and granzymes, leading to apoptosis of the target cells. Differentiate this from antibody-mediated rejection (ADCC, CDC, Type III), chronic rejection, or other forms of Type IV hypersensitivity. Remember that CTLs are crucial for eliminating cells presenting foreign MHC class I antigens.

<a id='question-115'></a>

### Question 115: Cell-Mediated Immunity: Part 3 Focus 15 (lymphocytes,)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the role of different T lymphocyte subsets (CD4+ helper T cells, CD8+ cytotoxic T cells, regulatory T cells) in immune responses, particularly in the context of graft rejection and the specific cytokines they produce. (理解不同 T 淋巴细胞亚群（CD4+ 辅助 T 细胞、CD8+ 细胞毒性 T 细胞、调节性 T 细胞）在免疫应答中的作用，特别是在移植物排斥和它们产生的特定细胞因子背景下。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with a history of chronic kidney disease receives a kidney transplant from a deceased donor. He is started on standard immunosuppressive therapy including cyclosporine, mycophenolate mofetil, and prednisone. Three months post-transplant, he develops fever, chills, flank pain, and decreased urine output. Serum creatinine rises from a baseline of 1.2 mg/dL to 4.5 mg/dL. A renal biopsy reveals acute tubular necrosis and interstitial inflammation with mononuclear cell infiltrates. Flow cytometry analysis of the biopsy tissue shows a significant increase in CD8+ T cells expressing granzyme B and perforin, along with CD4+ T cells producing IFN-γ and TNF-α. His cyclosporine level is found to be subtherapeutic. Which of the following is the MOST likely mechanism driving the acute rejection episode in this patient?

(A) Antibody-mediated complement activation leading to endothelial injury.
(B) CD4+ T helper cells secreting IL-2, promoting B cell proliferation and antibody production against donor antigens.
(C) CD8+ cytotoxic T lymphocytes directly killing graft parenchymal cells and tubular epithelial cells.
(D) Regulatory T cells (Tregs) failing to suppress the immune response against donor MHC class II molecules presented by recipient antigen-presenting cells.
(E) CD4+ T helper cells secreting IL-4, promoting eosinophil recruitment and mast cell degranulation.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male, kidney transplant recipient. This sets the stage for potential graft rejection.
- **Immunosuppression:** Standard triple therapy (cyclosporine, mycophenolate, prednisone) is used, but cyclosporine level is subtherapeutic. This suggests inadequate immunosuppression, increasing the risk of rejection.
- **Symptoms:** Fever, chills, flank pain, decreased urine output, rising creatinine. These are classic signs of acute graft dysfunction, highly suggestive of acute rejection.
- **Biopsy Findings:** Acute tubular necrosis, interstitial inflammation with mononuclear cell infiltrates. This confirms acute cellular rejection.
- **Flow Cytometry:** Increased CD8+ T cells expressing granzyme B and perforin, and CD4+ T cells producing IFN-γ and TNF-α. This points directly to cell-mediated cytotoxicity (CD8+ T cells) and inflammation (CD4+ T cells) as the primary mechanism. Granzyme B and perforin are key mediators of cytotoxic T lymphocyte (CTL)-mediated cell killing. IFN-γ and TNF-α are pro-inflammatory cytokines produced by activated T cells, particularly Th1 cells (often CD4+).
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the MOST likely mechanism driving the acute rejection.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Acute Cellular Rejection:** This is the most common type of acute rejection, primarily mediated by T lymphocytes recognizing donor MHC antigens (alloantigens) presented by recipient antigen-presenting cells (APCs).
- **T Cell Subsets:**
    - **CD8+ Cytotoxic T Lymphocytes (CTLs):** Recognize donor MHC class I molecules on graft cells (e.g., tubular epithelial cells, endothelial cells). Upon activation, they directly kill these cells via perforin/granzyme pathways or Fas/FasL interactions. The biopsy findings (increased CD8+ cells with granzyme B/perforin) strongly support this mechanism.
    - **CD4+ Helper T Lymphocytes (Th cells):** Recognize donor MHC class II molecules on recipient APCs. They primarily orchestrate the immune response by secreting cytokines. Th1 cells (producing IFN-γ, TNF-α) amplify the cell-mediated response, activating macrophages and enhancing CTL activity. The biopsy findings (increased CD4+ cells producing IFN-γ/TNF-α) support this role.
- **Cytokines:** IFN-γ is a key cytokine in cell-mediated immunity, activating macrophages and upregulating MHC expression. TNF-α is a potent pro-inflammatory cytokine.
- **Mechanism in this Case:** The subtherapeutic cyclosporine level allowed T cells to become activated. Both CD4+ (Th1) and CD8+ T cells were activated, as evidenced by the biopsy. CD8+ T cells directly mediate cytotoxicity against graft cells, while CD4+ T cells amplify the inflammatory response. The combination leads to acute cellular rejection characterized by inflammation and cell death (tubular necrosis).

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Antibody-mediated complement activation leading to endothelial injury. This describes antibody-mediated rejection (AMR), also known as humoral rejection. While possible, the biopsy findings (predominantly mononuclear cell infiltrates, increased CD8+ and CD4+ T cells with cytotoxic/inflammatory markers) are more characteristic of cellular rejection. AMR typically involves donor-specific antibodies (DSAs), C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries, and endothelialitis. This vignette lacks evidence for AMR.
- (B): CD4+ T helper cells secreting IL-2, promoting B cell proliferation and antibody production against donor antigens. IL-2 is primarily produced by activated T cells (both CD4+ and CD8+) and promotes T cell proliferation, not primarily B cell proliferation and antibody production. While CD4+ T cells help B cells, the primary mechanism described here (direct cytotoxicity by CD8+ T cells and inflammation driven by both CD4+ and CD8+ T cells) is more consistent with the findings. This option confuses the roles of cytokines and T cell subsets.
- (D): Regulatory T cells (Tregs) failing to suppress the immune response against donor MHC class II molecules presented by recipient antigen-presenting cells. Tregs (typically CD4+CD25+FoxP3+) are crucial for maintaining immune tolerance and preventing rejection. Their failure *could* contribute to rejection, but the vignette provides direct evidence of *active* cytotoxic and inflammatory T cell responses (increased CD8+ CTLs, IFN-γ/TNF-α producing CD4+ cells). While Treg dysfunction might be a factor, the *primary mechanism* driving the observed pathology is the effector T cell response itself, not just the failure of suppression. Option C describes the direct effector mechanism more accurately based on the provided data.
- (E): CD4+ T helper cells secreting IL-4, promoting eosinophil recruitment and mast cell degranulation. IL-4 is characteristic of Th2 responses, which are typically associated with allergic reactions and defense against helminths. Th2 responses are generally *not* the primary drivers of acute graft rejection; Th1 responses (IFN-γ, TNF-α) are dominant. Eosinophils and mast cells are not typical features of acute cellular rejection.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature             | Acute Cellular Rejection (Correct Answer C) | Antibody-Mediated Rejection (Distractor A) | Th2 Response (Distractor E) | Treg Failure (Distractor D) |
|----------------------|---------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------|-----------------------------|-----------------------------|
| **Primary Mediators** | CD8+ CTLs, CD4+ Th1 cells                  | Donor-Specific Antibodies (DSAs), Complement | CD4+ Th2 cells, Eosinophils | Failure of Treg suppression |
| **Key Cells**        | CD8+ T cells (CTLs), CD4+ T cells (Th1)     | B cells, Plasma cells, Endothelial cells | CD4+ T cells (Th2), Eosinophils | Tregs (CD4+CD25+FoxP3+)    |
| **Key Cytokines**    | IFN-γ, TNF-α, IL-2                          | -                                        | IL-4, IL-5, IL-13            | -                           |
| **Key Molecules**    | Perforin, Granzyme B, FasL                  | C4d, Antibodies, Complement components   | IgE                        | -                           |
| **Biopsy Findings**  | Mononuclear infiltrates, Tubular necrosis   | Endothelialitis, C4d deposition          | Eosinophilic infiltrates    | Variable, often increased inflammation |
| **Mechanism**        | Direct cell killing, Inflammation           | Complement activation, Endothelial injury | Allergic inflammation       | Loss of immune tolerance   |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Acute cellular rejection is primarily mediated by T lymphocytes (CD8+ CTLs and CD4+ Th1 cells). CD8+ T cells directly kill graft cells, while CD4+ T cells orchestrate the response via cytokine production (IFN-γ, TNF-α). Differentiating cellular from antibody-mediated rejection is crucial; cellular rejection involves T cell infiltrates and cytotoxicity, while antibody-mediated rejection involves DSAs, complement deposition (C4d), and endothelial injury. Th2 responses (IL-4) are typically associated with allergy, not graft rejection. Treg failure can contribute to rejection but isn't the direct effector mechanism described in the vignette.

<a id='question-116'></a>

### Question 116: Antibody Structure and Function
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the structure of immunoglobulins (antibodies), specifically the Fab and Fc regions, and their respective functions in antigen binding and effector mechanisms (e.g., complement activation, Fc receptor binding). (理解免疫球蛋白（抗体）的结构，特别是Fab区和Fc区，以及它们在抗原结合和效应机制中的各自功能，例如补体激活、Fc受体结合。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old male with a history of chronic lymphocytic leukemia (CLL) presents to the emergency department with fever, chills, and shortness of breath. He was recently treated with rituximab, an anti-CD20 monoclonal antibody, for his CLL. Physical examination reveals diffuse rhonchi and crackles on auscultation. Chest X-ray shows bilateral interstitial infiltrates. Sputum Gram stain reveals numerous Gram-negative bacilli. Sputum culture grows *Pseudomonas aeruginosa*. The patient is started on intravenous piperacillin-tazobactam. Despite broad-spectrum antibiotic therapy, his respiratory status deteriorates rapidly, leading to acute respiratory distress syndrome (ARDS). Subsequent laboratory tests show a significant decrease in serum complement C3 and C4 levels, while his IgG levels remain normal. Flow cytometry analysis of his peripheral blood lymphocytes reveals a marked reduction in CD19+ B cells, consistent with his underlying CLL and recent rituximab treatment. The patient's clinical condition continues to worsen, suggesting an unusual host response to the *Pseudomonas aeruginosa* infection.

Which of the following mechanisms is MOST likely contributing to the patient's severe and refractory pneumonia caused by *Pseudomonas aeruginosa*?

(A) Opsonization of *Pseudomonas aeruginosa* by the patient's remaining IgG antibodies, leading to enhanced phagocytosis by neutrophils.
(B) Complement-dependent cytotoxicity (CDC) mediated by the patient's remaining IgG antibodies binding to *Pseudomonas aeruginosa* surface antigens, leading to bacterial lysis.
(C) Antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) mediated by the patient's remaining IgG antibodies binding to *Pseudomonas aeruginosa* surface antigens and engaging FcγRIII (CD16) on NK cells, leading to bacterial killing.
(D) Activation of the alternative complement pathway by *Pseudomonas aeruginosa* lipopolysaccharide (LPS) binding directly to C3b, leading to opsonization and inflammation.
(E) Activation of the classical complement pathway by IgG antibodies bound to *Pseudomonas aeruginosa* surface antigens, leading to C3 convertase formation, opsonization, and inflammation.

#### Correct Answer: E

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 62-year-old male with CLL, treated with rituximab (anti-CD20). This indicates B-cell depletion and likely reduced antibody production, although IgG levels are reported as normal (this could be misleading or represent residual antibodies).
- **Presentation:** Fever, chills, shortness of breath, rhonchi/crackles, bilateral interstitial infiltrates -> Pneumonia.
- **Pathogen:** *Pseudomonas aeruginosa* (Gram-negative bacillus).
- **Treatment:** Piperacillin-tazobactam (broad-spectrum antibiotic).
- **Outcome:** Deteriorating respiratory status, ARDS despite antibiotics. This suggests an unusual or overwhelming host response, potentially immune-mediated.
- **Lab Findings:** Decreased C3/C4 (complement consumption), normal IgG. This points towards complement activation.
- **Flow Cytometry:** Reduced CD19+ B cells (consistent with CLL and rituximab).
- **Question:** Mechanism contributing to severe, refractory pneumonia.

The key clues are the *Pseudomonas aeruginosa* infection, the patient's history of CLL and rituximab treatment (implying B-cell depletion/dysfunction), the consumption of complement components (low C3/C4), and the severe, refractory nature of the pneumonia despite antibiotics. This constellation suggests that the host's immune response, specifically involving antibodies and complement, is playing a significant role, potentially contributing to the pathology (e.g., ARDS) rather than just clearing the infection.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Antibody Structure:** IgG antibodies have two main regions: Fab (Fragment antigen-binding) and Fc (Fragment crystallizable). The Fab region binds specifically to antigens. The Fc region mediates effector functions, including binding to Fc receptors on immune cells (like neutrophils, macrophages, NK cells) and activating the complement system.
- **Complement Activation:** The complement system can be activated via three pathways: classical, alternative, and lectin.
    - **Classical Pathway:** Triggered by antigen-antibody complexes (IgG or IgM binding to antigen). C1q binds to the Fc region of IgG (or IgM) bound to the antigen. This initiates a cascade leading to C3 convertase (C4b2a) and C5 convertase (C4b2a3b) formation, resulting in opsonization (C3b), inflammation (C3a, C5a), and cell lysis (MAC formation).
    - **Alternative Pathway:** Triggered by direct binding of C3b to microbial surfaces (like LPS) or other foreign materials. It bypasses the need for antibodies.
    - **Lectin Pathway:** Triggered by mannose-binding lectin (MBL) binding to mannose residues on microbial surfaces.
- ***Pseudomonas aeruginosa* and Immunity:** *P. aeruginosa* is an opportunistic pathogen. It possesses LPS (an endotoxin) that can activate the alternative complement pathway. It also has various virulence factors, including capsules, pili, and toxins.
- **Patient's Condition:** The patient has CLL and was treated with rituximab, leading to B-cell depletion. However, his IgG levels are reported as normal. This could mean:
    1.  The IgG levels were measured before the full effect of rituximab or represent residual antibodies.
    2.  The patient has a compensatory mechanism or a specific type of IgG response.
    3.  The "normal" IgG level is relative or misleading in the context of the severe infection and complement consumption.
- **Complement Consumption:** The low C3 and C4 levels strongly indicate activation and consumption of the classical complement pathway.
- **Connecting the Dots:** The classical pathway requires antigen-antibody complexes (specifically IgG or IgM). Given the *Pseudomonas* infection and the low C3/C4, it's highly likely that IgG antibodies are binding to *Pseudomonas* antigens and triggering the classical complement pathway. This leads to opsonization (C3b coating the bacteria, enhancing phagocytosis), inflammation (C3a, C5a attracting neutrophils), and potentially MAC formation (leading to bacterial lysis). The severe ARDS could be partly driven by the intense inflammatory response triggered by complement activation.

##### 3. Evaluating the Options
- **(A) Opsonization by IgG:** While IgG *does* opsonize, this option doesn't explain the complement consumption (low C3/C4). Opsonization alone doesn't typically consume complement to this extent. Furthermore, the severe, refractory nature suggests more than just enhanced phagocytosis.
- **(B) CDC mediated by IgG:** CDC is a function of the classical pathway activated by IgG-antigen complexes. This aligns with the low C3/C4 levels. However, the question asks for the mechanism *contributing* to the severe pneumonia. While CDC contributes to bacterial killing, the intense inflammation and potential tissue damage associated with classical pathway activation (especially in the context of ARDS) are also significant factors. Option (E) encompasses both opsonization and CDC as downstream effects of classical pathway activation.
- **(C) ADCC mediated by IgG:** ADCC involves IgG binding to target cells (or bacteria) and engaging FcγRIII (CD16) on NK cells. While possible, ADCC is less likely to be the primary driver of the severe pneumonia and complement consumption compared to classical pathway activation. NK cells primarily target virus-infected cells or tumor cells, although they can participate in antibacterial responses. The low C3/C4 strongly points towards complement activation.
- **(D) Alternative pathway activation by LPS:** *Pseudomonas* LPS *can* activate the alternative pathway. However, this pathway does *not* involve IgG binding and does *not* consume C4. The consumption of both C3 and C4 specifically points to the classical pathway.
- **(E) Classical pathway activation by IgG:** This mechanism directly explains the consumption of C3 and C4. IgG antibodies binding to *Pseudomonas* surface antigens initiate the classical pathway, leading to C3 convertase formation (using C4 and C2), opsonization (C3b), inflammation (C3a, C5a), and potentially MAC formation. This intense inflammatory response, driven by complement activation, is a plausible contributor to the patient's severe ARDS, especially in a potentially immunocompromised host. This option best integrates the clinical findings (infection, low C3/C4) and the known function of IgG.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- **(A):** Incorrect. While IgG opsonization occurs, it doesn't explain the significant consumption of C3 and C4, which is a hallmark of classical pathway activation. The patient's severe condition suggests more than just enhanced phagocytosis.
- **(B):** Incorrect. CDC is a consequence of classical pathway activation, but option (E) describes the initiating event (classical pathway activation by IgG) which leads to both CDC and opsonization, and explains the C3/C4 consumption more comprehensively. Option (E) is the more fundamental mechanism explaining the observed lab findings.
- **(C):** Incorrect. ADCC is mediated by IgG and FcγRIII on NK cells. While NK cells can contribute to antibacterial immunity, ADCC is not the primary mechanism explaining the consumption of C3 and C4. The clinical picture points strongly towards complement activation.
- **(D):** Incorrect. The alternative pathway is activated by direct binding of complement components to microbial surfaces (like LPS) and does *not* require antibodies (IgG) and does *not* consume C4. The consumption of both C3 and C4 indicates classical pathway activation.
- **(E):** Correct. This option accurately describes the mechanism that explains the consumption of both C3 and C4 (classical pathway activation) initiated by IgG antibodies binding to *Pseudomonas aeruginosa*. This activation leads to downstream effects like opsonization, CDC, and inflammation, which can contribute to the severe clinical presentation, including ARDS.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Classical Pathway Activation by IgG (Correct) | Alternative Pathway Activation by LPS | ADCC (IgG + NK cells) | Opsonization by IgG | CDC (IgG + Complement) |
| :---------------------- | :------------------------------------------ | :------------------------------------ | :------------------- | :------------------ | :------------------- |
| **Trigger**             | IgG-antigen complex                         | Microbial surface (e.g., LPS)         | IgG-target cell     | IgG-antigen         | IgG-antigen complex |
| **IgG Required?**       | Yes                                         | No                                    | Yes                  | Yes                 | Yes                  |
| **C3 Consumption?**     | Yes                                         | Yes                                   | No (directly)        | No (directly)       | Yes                  |
| **C4 Consumption?**     | Yes                                         | No                                    | No                   | No                  | Yes                  |
| **Primary Effector**    | Complement cascade, inflammation, opsonization | Opsonization, inflammation            | NK cell cytotoxicity | Phagocytosis        | Bacterial lysis      |
| **Relevance to Case**   | Explains low C3/C4, IgG role                 | *P. aeruginosa* LPS can activate, but doesn't explain low C4 | Less likely primary mechanism | Occurs, but doesn't explain low C3/C4 | Consequence of (E)    |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The key takeaway is understanding the specific roles of the Fab and Fc regions of IgG and how IgG binding to antigens triggers the classical complement pathway, leading to consumption of C3 and C4. Differentiating classical vs. alternative complement activation based on C3/C4 consumption is crucial. Remember that IgG is required for classical pathway activation, while the alternative pathway is antibody-independent. Differential diagnosis includes alternative pathway activation (no C4 consumption), ADCC (different effector cells/receptors), and simple opsonization (no significant complement consumption).

<a id='question-117'></a>

### Question 117: Cell-Mediated Immunity: Part 3 Focus 17 (circulation)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the role of specific lymphocyte subsets (specifically, circulating memory T cells) in secondary immune responses and their identification via surface markers, particularly in the context of graft rejection. (理解特定淋巴细胞亚群（特别是循环记忆T细胞）在继发免疫应答中的作用及其通过表面标志物的鉴定，特别是在移植物排斥的背景下。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 45-year-old male with a history of chronic kidney disease receives a kidney transplant from a deceased donor. He is maintained on immunosuppressive therapy including tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, and prednisone. Three months post-transplant, he develops fever, flank pain, and decreased urine output. Renal biopsy reveals acute tubular necrosis and interstitial inflammation with lymphocytic infiltration. Flow cytometry analysis of the peripheral blood shows an increased percentage of CD3+ cells expressing CD45RA- and CD62L-. Additionally, there is an elevated level of serum creatinine. Which of the following cell types is most likely responsible for the acute rejection episode in this patient?

(A) Naive T cells (CD3+CD45RA+CD62L+)
(B) Effector memory T cells (CD3+CD45RA-CD62L-)
(C) Central memory T cells (CD3+CD45RA-CD62L+)
(D) Regulatory T cells (CD3+CD4+CD25+)
(E) B cells (CD19+)

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 45-year-old male, kidney transplant recipient, chronic kidney disease history. This sets the stage for potential graft rejection.
- **Immunosuppression:** Tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, prednisone. This indicates an attempt to prevent rejection, but rejection can still occur.
- **Symptoms:** Fever, flank pain, decreased urine output 3 months post-transplant. These are classic signs of acute graft rejection.
- **Biopsy Findings:** Acute tubular necrosis, interstitial inflammation with lymphocytic infiltration. Confirms acute rejection process involving immune cells attacking the graft.
- **Flow Cytometry:** Increased percentage of CD3+ cells (T cells) expressing CD45RA- and CD62L-. This is the crucial immunological clue.
    - CD3+ identifies T cells.
    - CD45RA- indicates memory phenotype (as opposed to naive CD45RA+).
    - CD62L- indicates effector memory phenotype (as opposed to central memory CD62L+).
- **Elevated Creatinine:** Confirms worsening kidney function due to rejection.
- **Question Stem:** Asks which cell type is *most likely responsible* for the acute rejection episode, linking the clinical presentation and flow cytometry findings.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **T Cell Subsets:** T cells differentiate into various subsets based on their function and location. Key markers include CD45RA (isoform of protein tyrosine phosphatase, receptor type C) and CD62L (L-selectin).
    - **Naive T cells:** CD3+CD45RA+CD62L+. Circulate in blood and secondary lymphoid organs, have not encountered antigen.
    - **Central Memory T cells (Tcm):** CD3+CD45RA-CD62L+. Reside primarily in secondary lymphoid organs, respond rapidly to re-exposure to antigen, high proliferative capacity.
    - **Effector Memory T cells (Tem):** CD3+CD45RA-CD62L-. Circulate in blood and tissues, provide rapid effector function upon re-exposure to antigen, lower proliferative capacity than Tcm.
    - **Effector T cells:** CD3+CD45RA-CD62L-. Actively involved in immune responses, e.g., cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs) or Th1 cells.
    - **Regulatory T cells (Tregs):** CD3+CD4+CD25+FoxP3+. Suppress immune responses.
- **Graft Rejection:** An immune response against foreign MHC molecules on the transplanted organ. Acute rejection is typically T cell-mediated (cellular rejection) or antibody-mediated (humoral rejection). Cellular rejection involves cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs, CD8+) and helper T cells (CD4+) recognizing donor MHC molecules.
- **Memory T Cells in Rejection:** Memory T cells (both Tcm and Tem) are crucial for rapid and robust secondary immune responses, including graft rejection. They are pre-primed and can quickly differentiate into effector cells upon encountering the specific antigen (donor MHC).
- **Flow Cytometry Interpretation:** The patient's increased CD3+CD45RA-CD62L+ cells are characteristic of *effector memory T cells (Tem)*. These cells are primed to rapidly migrate to tissues and exert effector functions, making them key players in acute cellular graft rejection. While CD8+ T cells are the primary effectors in direct cellular rejection, CD4+ T cells (both helper and memory) also contribute significantly by providing help and recognizing donor MHC class II. The phenotype described (CD45RA-CD62L-) is typical for circulating memory cells poised for effector function.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Naive T cells (CD3+CD45RA+CD62L+):** Incorrect. Naive T cells have not encountered antigen and are not typically involved in secondary rejection episodes. They express CD45RA+ and CD62L+. The patient's cells are CD45RA-.
- (C) **Central memory T cells (CD3+CD45RA-CD62L+):** Incorrect. Central memory T cells are CD45RA- but express CD62L+. They primarily reside in lymphoid organs and are less involved in immediate effector functions in peripheral tissues like the transplanted kidney compared to effector memory cells. The patient's cells are CD62L-.
- (D) **Regulatory T cells (CD3+CD4+CD25+):** Incorrect. Regulatory T cells suppress immune responses and would *prevent* rejection, not cause it. While they play a role in transplant tolerance, they are not the primary effector cells in acute rejection. Their phenotype is CD3+CD4+CD25+ (and FoxP3+), not CD45RA-CD62L-.
- (E) **B cells (CD19+):** Incorrect. B cells are involved in humoral immunity (antibody production). While antibodies can contribute to rejection (antibody-mediated rejection), the primary cellular infiltrate described (lymphocytic infiltration) and the specific T cell phenotype identified by flow cytometry point towards a T cell-mediated process. B cells are identified by CD19+, not CD3+.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature             | Naive T cells (A) | Effector Memory T cells (B) | Central Memory T cells (C) | Regulatory T cells (D) | B cells (E) |
|----------------------|--------------------|-----------------------------|----------------------------|-------------------------|-------------|
| **CD3 Expression**   | +                  | +                           | +                          | +                       | -           |
| **CD45RA Expression**| +                  | -                           | -                          | Variable (often +)     | -           |
| **CD62L Expression** | +                  | -                           | +                          | Variable                | -           |
| **Location**         | Blood, Lymph Nodes | Blood, Tissues               | Lymph Nodes, Spleen        | Blood, Tissues          | Blood, Lymphoid Organs |
| **Function**         | Antigen Naive      | Rapid Effector Function     | Rapid Recall, Proliferation | Immune Suppression      | Antibody Production |
| **Role in Rejection**| No                 | Key Player                  | Contributes                 | Suppresses             | Contributes (Humoral) |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The key takeaway is recognizing the phenotype of effector memory T cells (CD45RA-CD62L-) as crucial mediators of rapid secondary immune responses, including acute graft rejection. Differentiating memory T cell subsets based on CD45RA and CD62L expression is essential. Differential diagnoses for graft rejection include acute vs. chronic rejection, cellular vs. humoral rejection, and infection mimicking rejection. Understanding the specific roles of different T cell subsets (naive, central memory, effector memory, regulatory) is critical for immunology.

<a id='question-118'></a>

### Question 118: Cell-Mediated Immunity: Part 3 Focus 18 (recirculate)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the role of different T cell subsets (Th1, Th2, Th17, Treg) and their associated cytokines in orchestrating immune responses, particularly in the context of intracellular pathogens and chronic inflammation. (理解不同T细胞亚群（Th1、Th2、Th17、Treg）及其相关细胞因子在协调免疫反应中的作用，特别是在细胞内病原体和慢性炎症的背景下。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 32-year-old male presents to the emergency department with a 3-week history of progressively worsening cough, fever, night sweats, and weight loss. He is HIV-positive with a CD4+ T cell count of 85 cells/µL. Chest X-ray reveals bilateral interstitial infiltrates. Sputum analysis is negative for acid-fast bacilli (AFB) on initial smear but positive on culture after 6 weeks. The culture grows a slow-growing mycobacterium that is catalase-positive, niacin-positive, and does not grow in the presence of sulfamethoxazole. Bronchoalveolar lavage (BAL) fluid analysis shows a significant increase in CD4+ T cells compared to peripheral blood, but these cells exhibit impaired proliferation in response to stimulation with purified protein derivative (PPD). The patient is started on a multi-drug anti-tuberculosis regimen. Despite treatment, his respiratory symptoms worsen, and he develops disseminated disease involving the liver and bone marrow. Further immunological testing reveals a marked deficiency in the production of interferon-gamma (IFN-γ) by his T cells upon stimulation.

Which of the following best describes the primary immunological defect contributing to this patient's susceptibility and poor response to treatment for this infection?

(A) Impaired activation of B cells leading to deficient antibody production against mycobacterial antigens.
(B) Defective differentiation and function of cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs) responsible for killing infected cells.
(C) Reduced production of interleukin-4 (IL-4) by T cells, impairing macrophage activation and granuloma formation.
(D) Impaired differentiation of T helper 1 (Th1) cells and subsequent reduced production of IFN-γ, crucial for macrophage activation and control of intracellular pathogens.
(E) Excessive production of interleukin-17 (IL-17) by Th17 cells, leading to uncontrolled neutrophilic inflammation and tissue damage.

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Profile:** 32-year-old male, HIV-positive, CD4+ count 85 cells/µL (severe immunosuppression). This immediately points towards opportunistic infections, particularly those typically controlled by cell-mediated immunity.
*   **Symptoms:** Chronic cough, fever, night sweats, weight loss (classic symptoms of tuberculosis or other chronic infections).
*   **Radiology:** Bilateral interstitial infiltrates (consistent with disseminated mycobacterial infection).
*   **Microbiology:** Sputum culture positive for slow-growing mycobacterium, catalase-positive, niacin-positive, does not grow in sulfamethoxazole. This profile strongly suggests *Mycobacterium tuberculosis*. AFB smear negative initially is common in HIV patients.
*   **Immunology:** Increased CD4+ T cells in BAL (suggesting recruitment to the site of infection), but impaired proliferation in response to PPD (indicating defective T cell function). Marked deficiency in IFN-γ production by T cells upon stimulation.
*   **Clinical Course:** Worsening symptoms and dissemination despite anti-tuberculosis treatment. This points to a profound immune defect hindering pathogen control.
*   **Question Stem:** Asks for the primary immunological defect.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
*   *Mycobacterium tuberculosis* is an intracellular pathogen primarily controlled by cell-mediated immunity, specifically Th1 responses.
*   **Th1 Response:** Upon encountering *M. tuberculosis*, antigen-presenting cells (APCs) like macrophages and dendritic cells present mycobacterial antigens via MHC class II to CD4+ T cells. In the presence of IL-12 (produced by APCs), naive CD4+ T cells differentiate into Th1 cells.
*   **Th1 Cytokines:** Th1 cells produce key cytokines, most importantly **Interferon-gamma (IFN-γ)** and Tumor Necrosis Factor-alpha (TNF-α).
*   **IFN-γ Function:** IFN-γ is crucial for activating macrophages, enhancing their phagocytic and microbicidal activity (e.g., production of reactive oxygen species, nitric oxide). Activated macrophages are essential for killing intracellular *M. tuberculosis*. IFN-γ also promotes the formation and maintenance of granulomas, which wall off the infection.
*   **HIV and Th1 Response:** HIV primarily infects and destroys CD4+ T cells, including Th1 cells. In advanced HIV infection (low CD4 count), the Th1 response is severely impaired, leading to deficient IFN-γ production. This inability to activate macrophages effectively allows *M. tuberculosis* to proliferate unchecked, leading to active disease and dissemination. The patient's low CD4 count (85 cells/µL) and deficient IFN-γ production directly correlate with this impaired Th1 response.
*   **Other T cell subsets:** Th2 cells (produce IL-4, IL-5, IL-13) are primarily involved in humoral immunity and defense against extracellular parasites; they generally inhibit Th1 responses. Th17 cells (produce IL-17) are involved in defense against extracellular bacteria and fungi and contribute to inflammation. Treg cells (produce IL-10, TGF-β) suppress immune responses. While other T cell subsets play roles, the primary defect in controlling *M. tuberculosis* is the failure of the Th1 response.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) Impaired activation of B cells leading to deficient antibody production against mycobacterial antigens. While antibodies can play a role, *M. tuberculosis* is primarily controlled by cell-mediated immunity. The main defect in this patient is T cell dysfunction, not B cell dysfunction. Antibody responses to *M. tuberculosis* are generally considered less critical for control compared to Th1 responses.
- (B) Defective differentiation and function of cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs) responsible for killing infected cells. CTLs (CD8+ T cells) recognize mycobacterial antigens presented on MHC class I molecules and can kill infected cells. While CTLs contribute to immunity against *M. tuberculosis*, the primary defect in severe HIV-associated TB is the failure of CD4+ Th1 cells to activate macrophages via IFN-γ. The vignette specifically highlights the impaired IFN-γ production by CD4+ T cells.
- (C) Reduced production of interleukin-4 (IL-4) by T cells, impairing macrophage activation and granuloma formation. IL-4 is characteristic of Th2 responses and generally *inhibits* Th1 responses and macrophage activation. A *reduction* in IL-4 would not explain the patient's susceptibility to *M. tuberculosis*; rather, an *excess* of IL-4 could potentially worsen the situation by suppressing Th1 immunity. The key cytokine deficiency here is IFN-γ, not IL-4.
- (E) Excessive production of interleukin-17 (IL-17) by Th17 cells, leading to uncontrolled neutrophilic inflammation and tissue damage. While IL-17 can contribute to inflammation in TB, excessive production is not the primary immunological defect leading to susceptibility and poor treatment response in advanced HIV. The core problem is the lack of IFN-γ driven macrophage activation.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | *Mycobacterium tuberculosis* Control | HIV-Associated TB (Patient) | Th1 Response | IFN-γ Role                                  |
| :---------------------- | :---------------------------------- | :-------------------------- | :----------- | :------------------------------------------ |
| Primary Immune Response | Cell-Mediated (Th1)                 | Severely Impaired           | Deficient    | Macrophage activation, granuloma formation |
| Key Cytokine            | IFN-γ                               | Deficient                  | Low          | Activates macrophages                       |
| Key Cell Type           | CD4+ Th1 cells, Activated Macrophages | Low CD4+ T cells            | Low          | Produces IFN-γ                              |
| Pathogen Control        | Effective                           | Ineffective                 | Impaired     | Essential for killing intracellular bacteria |
| Clinical Manifestation  | Latent or Active TB                 | Active, Disseminated TB     | N/A          | N/A                                         |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The primary defense against intracellular pathogens like *Mycobacterium tuberculosis* relies on a robust Th1 cell-mediated immune response, characterized by the production of IFN-γ, which activates macrophages. Severe immunosuppression, such as that seen in advanced HIV infection, leads to impaired Th1 differentiation and function, resulting in deficient IFN-γ production and inability to control the infection. Key differentials include other opportunistic infections in AIDS (e.g., *Pneumocystis jirovecii*, CMV), but the specific defect highlighted (impaired IFN-γ production) points directly to the failure of the Th1 pathway crucial for mycobacterial control.

<a id='question-119'></a>

### Question 119: Cell-Mediated Immunity: Part 3 Focus 19 (protect)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the role of CD8+ cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs) in recognizing and eliminating infected cells, specifically in the context of viral infections and their interaction with MHC class I molecules. (理解CD8+细胞毒性T淋巴细胞(CTLs)在识别和清除受感染细胞中的作用，特别是在病毒感染的背景下以及它们与MHC I类分子的相互作用。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 32-year-old male presents to the emergency department with a 3-day history of fever, myalgia, and a diffuse maculopapular rash. He is a known HIV-positive patient with a CD4+ T cell count of 150 cells/µL and an undetectable viral load due to highly active antiretroviral therapy (HAART). He reports no recent travel or sick contacts. Physical examination reveals generalized lymphadenopathy and hepatosplenomegaly. Laboratory tests show elevated liver enzymes (AST 350 U/L, ALT 400 U/L) and thrombocytopenia (platelet count 80,000/µL). A skin biopsy from the rash shows lymphocytic infiltrate surrounding degenerating hepatocytes. Flow cytometry analysis of the skin biopsy reveals a significant population of CD8+ T cells expressing granzyme B and perforin, closely associated with the degenerating hepatocytes. Which of the following is the most likely mechanism responsible for the hepatocyte damage in this patient?

(A) Antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) mediated by NK cells recognizing hepatocytes coated with viral antibodies.
(B) Complement-mediated lysis of hepatocytes due to deposition of immune complexes.
(C) Direct cytotoxicity mediated by CD8+ T lymphocytes recognizing viral peptides presented on MHC class I molecules on hepatocytes.
(D) Type III hypersensitivity reaction involving deposition of circulating immune complexes in the liver sinusoids.
(E) Macrophage-mediated phagocytosis of hepatocytes opsonized by antibodies and complement.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 32-year-old male, HIV-positive, on HAART, low CD4 count (150 cells/µL), undetectable viral load. This indicates significant immunosuppression despite HAART, suggesting potential for opportunistic infections or immune reconstitution inflammatory syndrome (IRIS), although the undetectable viral load makes active HIV replication less likely as the primary driver.
- **Symptoms:** Fever, myalgia, maculopapular rash, lymphadenopathy, hepatosplenomegaly. These are non-specific systemic symptoms often seen in viral infections or immune reactions.
- **Laboratory Findings:** Elevated liver enzymes (AST, ALT), thrombocytopenia. Suggests liver involvement (hepatitis) and potential bone marrow suppression or splenic sequestration.
- **Skin Biopsy:** Lymphocytic infiltrate surrounding degenerating hepatocytes. Points towards an immune-mediated process targeting the liver cells.
- **Flow Cytometry:** CD8+ T cells expressing granzyme B and perforin associated with degenerating hepatocytes. This is the crucial clue. CD8+ T cells are cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs). Granzyme B and perforin are key effector molecules used by CTLs to induce apoptosis in target cells. Their presence associated with degenerating hepatocytes strongly suggests CTL-mediated hepatocyte killing.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the mechanism responsible for hepatocyte damage.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Cell-Mediated Immunity (CMI):** CMI is primarily mediated by T lymphocytes. CD4+ T helper cells (Th) orchestrate immune responses, while CD8+ T cells (CTLs) directly kill infected or abnormal cells.
- **CTL Activation and Function:** CTLs recognize foreign antigens (e.g., viral peptides) presented on MHC class I molecules on the surface of target cells. This recognition, along with co-stimulatory signals, activates the CTL. Activated CTLs release cytotoxic granules containing perforin and granzymes. Perforin forms pores in the target cell membrane, allowing granzymes to enter and activate caspases, leading to apoptosis of the target cell.
- **MHC Class I:** Expressed on virtually all nucleated cells. Presents endogenous antigens (e.g., viral proteins synthesized within the cell) to CD8+ T cells.
- **HIV and CMI:** HIV primarily infects CD4+ T cells, leading to immunodeficiency. However, even with HAART and low viral load, residual immune dysregulation can occur. In this case, the patient's immunosuppression likely predisposes him to certain infections or immune reactions. The presence of CD8+ T cells expressing cytotoxic molecules targeting hepatocytes suggests a CTL response against hepatocytes, possibly due to viral infection (e.g., CMV, EBV, HSV reactivation) or an autoimmune process. The clinical picture is consistent with a drug reaction (HAART-related) or an opportunistic infection triggering an exaggerated CTL response against infected hepatocytes. Regardless of the trigger, the mechanism of hepatocyte damage is CTL-mediated cytotoxicity.
- **Option Analysis:**
    - (A) ADCC: Mediated by NK cells or other cytotoxic cells (like neutrophils, eosinophils) binding to Fc receptors on antibodies coating target cells. While possible in some viral infections, the flow cytometry specifically identifies CD8+ T cells with cytotoxic molecules targeting hepatocytes, making CTL-mediated killing the primary mechanism.
    - (B) Complement-mediated lysis: Involves activation of the complement cascade leading to formation of the membrane attack complex (MAC) or opsonization. While complement can be involved in some liver injuries, the specific finding of CTLs with perforin/granzyme targeting hepatocytes points away from this as the primary mechanism.
    - (C) CTL-mediated cytotoxicity: Directly aligns with the flow cytometry findings (CD8+ T cells, granzyme B, perforin) associated with degenerating hepatocytes. CTLs recognize viral antigens (or other abnormal antigens) presented on MHC class I molecules on hepatocytes and kill them. This is the most direct explanation for the observed pathology.
    - (D) Type III hypersensitivity: Involves deposition of circulating immune complexes (antigen-antibody complexes) in tissues, leading to complement activation and inflammation. While this can cause liver damage (e.g., serum sickness), it doesn't typically involve direct CTL killing of hepatocytes, and the flow cytometry findings are inconsistent with this mechanism.
    - (E) Macrophage-mediated phagocytosis: Macrophages phagocytose opsonized particles or cells. While macrophages can contribute to liver damage, the key finding here is the direct cytotoxic activity of CD8+ T cells against hepatocytes.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) is mediated by effector cells (like NK cells) recognizing antibody-coated target cells via Fc receptors. While NK cells can be involved in viral control, the vignette specifically points to CD8+ T cells expressing cytotoxic molecules directly targeting hepatocytes. This option confuses the effector cell type and mechanism.
- (B): Complement-mediated lysis involves the complement cascade forming the MAC. While complement can contribute to inflammation and tissue damage, the primary mechanism identified by the flow cytometry is direct cell killing by CTLs, not complement lysis.
- (D): Type III hypersensitivity involves immune complex deposition. This typically causes vasculitis or glomerulonephritis, and while it can affect the liver, it doesn't explain the specific finding of CTLs directly targeting hepatocytes with perforin and granzymes.
- (E): Macrophage-mediated phagocytosis requires opsonization (antibodies or complement). While macrophages are involved in clearing debris and can contribute to inflammation, the direct cytotoxic killing of hepatocytes by CTLs is the specific mechanism highlighted in the vignette.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | CTL-Mediated Cytotoxicity (Correct) | ADCC (Distractor A)             | Complement Lysis (Distractor B) | Type III Hypersensitivity (Distractor D) | Macrophage Phagocytosis (Distractor E) |
| :---------------------- | :---------------------------------- | :------------------------------ | :------------------------------- | :--------------------------------------- | :------------------------------------- |
| **Effector Cell**       | CD8+ T Lymphocyte                  | NK cell, Neutrophil, etc.       | Complement System                | Immune Complexes, Neutrophils           | Macrophage                             |
| **Target Recognition**  | MHC Class I + Peptide              | Fc Receptor + Antibody          | C3b, C5b-9 (MAC)                 | Immune Complex Deposition               | Opsonins (Ab, C3b)                     |
| **Mechanism**           | Perforin/Granzyme, FasL             | Perforin/Granzyme, ADCC receptors | MAC formation                   | Inflammation, Complement Activation      | Engulfment                             |
| **Key Molecules**       | CD8, TCR, Perforin, Granzyme B      | FcγR, Perforin, Granzyme B       | C3, C5, C6, C7, C8, C9            | IgG, IgM, C3                            | FcγR, CR1                              |
| **Vignette Support**    | Strong (Flow cytometry data)        | Weak                            | Weak                             | Weak                                     | Weak                                   |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
CD8+ cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs) are crucial for eliminating virus-infected cells and tumor cells by recognizing antigens presented on MHC class I molecules and inducing apoptosis via perforin and granzymes. Differentiating CTL-mediated cytotoxicity from other immune mechanisms like ADCC, complement lysis, or hypersensitivity reactions is essential. Key clues include the identification of CD8+ T cells expressing cytotoxic molecules directly associated with target cells.

### Question 120: Cell-Mediated Immunity: Part 3 Focus 20 (Figure)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the role of different T cell subsets (Th1, Th2, Th17, Treg) and their characteristic cytokine profiles in orchestrating immune responses, particularly in the context of intracellular pathogens and chronic inflammation. (理解不同T细胞亚群（Th1、Th2、Th17、Treg）及其特征性细胞因子谱在协调免疫应答中的作用，特别是在细胞内病原体和慢性炎症的背景下。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 35-year-old male presents to the clinic with a 3-month history of persistent cough, fever, night sweats, and weight loss. He is HIV-positive with a CD4+ T cell count of 85 cells/µL. Chest X-ray reveals bilateral interstitial infiltrates, and sputum analysis is positive for acid-fast bacilli (AFB). He is started on a multi-drug anti-tuberculosis regimen. Despite treatment, his symptoms worsen, and he develops disseminated disease involving the liver and bone marrow. Further investigation reveals that his macrophages are unable to effectively kill the intracellular *Mycobacterium tuberculosis*. Laboratory studies show significantly elevated levels of IL-10 and decreased levels of IFN-γ in his serum. Which of the following T cell subsets is most likely deficient in this patient, contributing to his inability to control the *Mycobacterium tuberculosis* infection?

(A) Th1 cells
(B) Th2 cells
(C) Th17 cells
(D) Treg cells
(E) Cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs)

#### Correct Answer: (A)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Profile:** 35-year-old male, HIV-positive, low CD4+ count (85 cells/µL) - indicates severe immunosuppression, particularly affecting cell-mediated immunity.
*   **Symptoms:** Persistent cough, fever, night sweats, weight loss - classic symptoms of tuberculosis (TB).
*   **Diagnosis:** Sputum positive for AFB, bilateral interstitial infiltrates - confirms active pulmonary TB.
*   **Complication:** Worsening symptoms despite treatment, disseminated disease - suggests impaired immune control of *M. tuberculosis*.
*   **Pathophysiology:** Macrophages unable to kill intracellular *M. tuberculosis*.
*   **Laboratory Findings:** Elevated IL-10, decreased IFN-γ - key immunological clues pointing towards the specific T cell subset involved.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
*   ***Mycobacterium tuberculosis* Infection:** An intracellular bacterium primarily controlled by cell-mediated immunity. Macrophages phagocytose the bacteria, but *M. tuberculosis* inhibits phagosome-lysosome fusion, allowing it to survive and replicate within macrophages.
*   **Normal Immune Response:** Effective control requires activation of macrophages by Th1 cells. Th1 cells differentiate in response to IL-12 (produced by macrophages and dendritic cells) and IFN-γ. Th1 cells produce IFN-γ, which activates macrophages to kill intracellular bacteria via reactive oxygen species (ROS) and nitric oxide (NO).
*   **Role of T cell subsets:**
    *   **Th1 cells:** Produce IFN-γ, crucial for activating macrophages against intracellular pathogens like *M. tuberculosis*. (产生IFN-γ，对抗如结核分枝杆菌的细胞内病原体至关重要。)
    *   **Th2 cells:** Produce IL-4, IL-5, IL-13, primarily involved in responses against extracellular parasites (helminths) and allergic reactions. They can suppress Th1 responses. (产生IL-4、IL-5、IL-13，主要参与对抗胞外寄生虫和过敏反应。它们可以抑制Th1反应。)
    *   **Th17 cells:** Produce IL-17, involved in defense against extracellular bacteria and fungi, particularly at mucosal surfaces. (产生IL-17，参与对抗胞外细菌和真菌，尤其是在粘膜表面。)
    *   **Treg cells:** Produce IL-10 and TGF-β, involved in suppressing immune responses to maintain tolerance and prevent autoimmunity. (产生IL-10和TGF-β，参与抑制免疫应答以维持耐受性并防止自身免疫。)
    *   **CTLs (CD8+ T cells):** Kill infected host cells, important for viral infections and tumor immunity. While they can contribute to TB control, the primary mechanism for killing intracellular *M. tuberculosis* within macrophages is macrophage activation by Th1 cells. (杀死受感染的宿主细胞，对抗病毒感染和肿瘤免疫很重要。虽然它们可以为结核控制做出贡献，但杀死巨噬细胞内结核分枝杆菌的主要机制是Th1细胞激活巨噬细胞。)
*   **Patient's Condition:** The patient has severe HIV infection (low CD4+ count), which impairs Th1 cell function. The elevated IL-10 (immunosuppressive cytokine produced by Treg cells and macrophages) and decreased IFN-γ (key Th1 cytokine) confirm a deficiency in Th1-mediated macrophage activation. This failure allows *M. tuberculosis* to persist and disseminate despite antibiotic treatment.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (B) **Th2 cells:** While Th2 responses can sometimes inhibit Th1 responses, a deficiency in Th2 cells would not directly explain the failure to control an intracellular pathogen like *M. tuberculosis*. Th2 responses are more associated with allergic diseases and helminth infections. The patient's clinical picture and lab findings (low IFN-γ) point away from a primary Th2 deficiency. (Th2反应有时会抑制Th1反应，但Th2细胞缺乏不能直接解释无法控制如结核分枝杆菌的细胞内病原体。Th2反应更多地与过敏性疾病和蠕虫感染相关。患者的临床表现和实验室发现（低IFN-γ）表明这不是Th2细胞缺乏。)
- (C) **Th17 cells:** Th17 cells are primarily involved in defense against extracellular bacteria and fungi, particularly at mucosal surfaces. While they play some role in TB immunity, the primary defect in controlling intracellular *M. tuberculosis* within macrophages is the lack of Th1-mediated IFN-γ production and subsequent macrophage activation. (Th17细胞主要参与对抗胞外细菌和真菌，尤其是在粘膜表面。虽然它们在结核免疫中发挥一些作用，但控制巨噬细胞内结核分枝杆菌的主要缺陷是缺乏Th1介导的IFN-γ产生和随后的巨噬细胞激活。)
- (D) **Treg cells:** Treg cells suppress immune responses. While elevated IL-10 (produced by Treg cells) is observed, the primary problem is the *lack* of an effective immune response (specifically Th1) against the pathogen, not excessive suppression. The deficiency is in the effector T cell subset (Th1) needed to activate macrophages, not in the regulatory subset. (Treg细胞抑制免疫应答。虽然观察到IL-10升高（由Treg细胞产生），但主要问题是缺乏对抗病原体的有效免疫应答（具体是Th1）。缺乏的是激活巨噬细胞所需的效应T细胞亚群（Th1），而不是调节亚群。)
- (E) **Cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs):** CTLs kill infected host cells. While CTLs can kill infected macrophages, the predominant mechanism for controlling *M. tuberculosis* involves Th1-mediated activation of macrophages to kill the intracellular bacteria. The patient's inability of macrophages to kill the bacteria, coupled with low IFN-γ, strongly points to a Th1 defect rather than a primary CTL defect. (CTL杀死受感染的宿主细胞。虽然CTL可以杀死受感染的巨噬细胞，但控制结核分枝杆菌的主要机制涉及Th1介导的巨噬细胞激活以杀死细胞内细菌。患者巨噬细胞无法杀死细菌，结合低IFN-γ，强烈表明存在Th1缺陷，而不是主要的CTL缺陷。)

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature             | Th1 Deficiency (Correct) | Th2 Deficiency | Th17 Deficiency | Treg Deficiency | CTL Deficiency |
| :------------------ | :----------------------- | :------------- | :-------------- | :-------------- | :------------- |
| **Primary Role**    | Intracellular pathogens  | Helminths, Allergies | Extracellular bacteria/fungi | Immune regulation | Virus-infected cells, Tumors |
| **Key Cytokine**    | IFN-γ (Low in vignette)  | IL-4, IL-5, IL-13 | IL-17           | IL-10, TGF-β (High IL-10 in vignette) | Granzyme B, Perforin |
| **Pathogen Control** | Macrophage activation    | Eosinophil activation | Neutrophil recruitment | Suppression of autoimmunity | Killing infected cells |
| **Associated Disease** | Susceptibility to intracellular bacteria (e.g., *M. tuberculosis*, *Listeria*) | Susceptibility to helminths | Susceptibility to extracellular bacteria/fungi | Autoimmunity, Immunodeficiency | Susceptibility to viruses, Tumors |
| **Vignette Fit**    | Yes (HIV, low IFN-γ, disseminated TB) | No             | No              | No              | No             |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The primary defense against intracellular pathogens like *Mycobacterium tuberculosis* relies on Th1 cells activating macrophages via IFN-γ production. Immunosuppression, particularly affecting CD4+ T cells (as seen in HIV), leads to impaired Th1 function and increased susceptibility to opportunistic intracellular infections. Key laboratory findings include low IFN-γ and elevated IL-10, indicating a failure of Th1-mediated immunity and potentially increased regulatory suppression. Differential diagnoses for disseminated TB in an immunocompromised host include other intracellular pathogens (*Listeria*, *Nocardia*) or opportunistic fungal infections, but the specific immunological profile points strongly to a Th1 defect.

<a id='question-121'></a>

### Question 121: Cell-Mediated Immunity: Part 3 Focus 21 (Migration)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the role of chemokines and chemokine receptors in leukocyte migration, specifically the migration of T cells to sites of inflammation or infection, and how disruption of this process can lead to immunodeficiency or altered immune responses. (理解趋化因子和趋化因子受体在白细胞迁移中的作用，特别是T细胞迁移到炎症或感染部位的过程，以及该过程的破坏如何导致免疫缺陷或免疫反应改变。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 35-year-old male presents to the emergency department with a 3-week history of persistent, non-productive cough, low-grade fever, and progressive dyspnea. He is HIV-positive with a CD4+ T cell count of 80 cells/µL. He reports no recent travel or known exposure to tuberculosis. Chest X-ray reveals bilateral interstitial infiltrates. Bronchoalveolar lavage (BAL) fluid analysis shows a significantly elevated number of lymphocytes, but cultures for typical bacterial pathogens (Streptococcus pneumoniae, Staphylococcus aureus, Haemophilus influenzae) and fungi (Pneumocystis jirovecii, Aspergillus) are negative. Flow cytometry of BAL lymphocytes reveals a predominance of CD4+ T cells expressing CXCR3, but lacking expression of CCR5. The patient is subsequently diagnosed with Pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia (PJP) despite negative cultures, confirmed by BAL staining. Given the patient's specific immune profile, which chemokine receptor is most likely deficient or non-functional, contributing to the impaired recruitment of immune cells to the lungs and the atypical presentation of PJP?

(A) CCR7
(B) CXCR4
(C) CCR5
(D) CXCR3
(E) CCR2

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Profile:** 35-year-old male, HIV-positive, CD4+ count 80 cells/µL (severe immunosuppression). This indicates a high risk for opportunistic infections.
*   **Symptoms:** Persistent cough, low-grade fever, dyspnea (suggestive of pulmonary infection).
*   **Imaging:** Bilateral interstitial infiltrates (common finding in PJP and other opportunistic lung infections).
*   **BAL Findings:** Elevated lymphocytes, negative bacterial/fungal cultures, positive PJP staining. This points towards an atypical presentation or a non-bacterial/fungal cause, confirmed as PJP.
*   **Flow Cytometry:** Predominance of CD4+ T cells expressing CXCR3 but lacking CCR5. This is the crucial clue. CXCR3 is a chemokine receptor important for T cell migration to sites of inflammation, particularly in response to chemokines like CXCL9, CXCL10, and CXCL11 produced during viral infections and by epithelial cells. CCR5 is a chemokine receptor crucial for the entry of HIV into CD4+ T cells (using CCR5 as a co-receptor) and also plays a role in T cell trafficking, particularly to sites of inflammation involving macrophages.
*   **Question Stem:** Asks which chemokine receptor is deficient/non-functional, contributing to impaired recruitment. The key is the flow cytometry finding: CD4+ T cells *lack* CCR5 expression.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
*   **Chemokine Receptors & T Cell Migration:** T cell migration to sites of infection or inflammation is a highly regulated process mediated by chemokines and their receptors on leukocytes.
    *   **CCR7:** Expressed on naive T cells and dendritic cells. Mediates migration towards CCL19 and CCL21 produced in secondary lymphoid organs (lymph nodes). Essential for initiating adaptive immune responses.
    *   **CCR5:** Expressed on monocytes, macrophages, dendritic cells, and a subset of T cells. Binds CCL3, CCL4, and CCL5. Important for trafficking to sites of inflammation, particularly those involving macrophages. Also the co-receptor for HIV entry into cells expressing CCR5 (along with CD4).
    *   **CXCR3:** Expressed on activated T cells (especially Th1 cells), NK cells, and some B cells. Binds CXCL9, CXCL10, and CXCL11. Mediates migration towards sites of inflammation, particularly during viral infections and IFN-γ-mediated responses.
    *   **CXCR4:** Expressed on hematopoietic stem cells, lymphocytes, and monocytes. Binds CXCL12 (SDF-1). Important for homing to bone marrow and lymphoid organs. Also a co-receptor for HIV entry (along with CD4).
    *   **CCR2:** Expressed on monocytes, macrophages, and some T cells. Binds CCL2 (MCP-1). Important for monocyte recruitment to sites of inflammation.
*   **HIV and CCR5:** HIV primarily uses either CCR5 or CXCR4 as a co-receptor with CD4 to enter target cells. Individuals homozygous for the CCR5-Δ32 mutation lack functional CCR5 receptors and are highly resistant to HIV infection via the CCR5-tropic route. Some HIV strains use CXCR4 (X4-tropic).
*   **Patient's Case:** The patient has severe HIV infection (low CD4 count) and PJP. The flow cytometry shows CD4+ T cells expressing CXCR3 (consistent with activation/inflammation) but lacking CCR5. The lack of CCR5 expression impairs the ability of these T cells to migrate to the lungs in response to chemokines like CCL5, which are produced during PJP infection. This impaired migration contributes to the ineffective immune response and potentially the atypical presentation (negative cultures despite staining). While the patient is infected with HIV, the question focuses on the *functional consequence* of the T cell phenotype (lack of CCR5) on immune cell migration in the context of PJP. The lack of CCR5 impairs trafficking to the site of infection.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
*   **(A) CCR7:** CCR7 is crucial for homing to lymph nodes. Deficiency would impair the initiation of adaptive immunity in secondary lymphoid organs, leading to broader immunodeficiency, not specifically impaired migration to the lungs in this context. While important for T cell trafficking, it's not the receptor specifically implicated by the flow cytometry data (lack of CCR5, presence of CXCR3).
*   **(B) CXCR4:** CXCR4 is primarily involved in homing to bone marrow and lymphoid organs via CXCL12. While important for lymphocyte circulation, its deficiency is not the primary reason for impaired migration to the lung parenchyma in response to PJP infection, and it is not the receptor specifically lacking in the patient's T cells according to the vignette.
*   **(C) CCR5:** This is the correct answer. The vignette explicitly states that the patient's CD4+ T cells *lack* CCR5 expression. CCR5 is involved in T cell trafficking to inflammatory sites, and its absence would impair the recruitment of these T cells to the lungs to combat the PJP infection. This aligns perfectly with the clinical presentation and the flow cytometry findings.
*   **(D) CXCR3:** The vignette states that the patient's CD4+ T cells *express* CXCR3. CXCR3 is important for migration to sites of inflammation, particularly in response to chemokines produced during viral infections. Its presence suggests the T cells are potentially capable of responding to certain inflammatory signals, but the *lack* of CCR5 is the key defect hindering overall recruitment.
*   **(E) CCR2:** CCR2 is primarily involved in monocyte recruitment via CCL2. While monocytes are involved in PJP, the question focuses on T cell migration, and the flow cytometry specifically describes the T cell phenotype (lack of CCR5, presence of CXCR3).

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature          | CCR7                               | CCR5                                  | CXCR3                                 | CXCR4                                 | CCR2                                  |
| :--------------- | :--------------------------------- | :------------------------------------ | :------------------------------------ | :------------------------------------ | :------------------------------------ |
| **Primary Role** | Lymph node homing (naive T cells) | T cell/Monocyte trafficking to inflammation, HIV co-receptor | Activated T cell trafficking to inflammation (viral) | Homing to bone marrow/lymphoid organs, HIV co-receptor | Monocyte recruitment to inflammation |
| **Ligands**      | CCL19, CCL21                        | CCL3, CCL4, CCL5                      | CXCL9, CXCL10, CXCL11                  | CXCL12 (SDF-1)                        | CCL2 (MCP-1)                          |
| **Deficiency Effect** | Impaired adaptive immunity initiation | Impaired trafficking to certain inflammatory sites, HIV susceptibility | Impaired trafficking to certain inflammatory sites (viral) | Impaired homing to bone marrow/lymphoid organs | Impaired monocyte recruitment         |
| **Vignette Finding** | Not relevant (T cells lack CCR5) | **T cells lack CCR5**                 | T cells express CXCR3                 | Not relevant (T cells lack CCR5) | Not relevant (T cells lack CCR5) |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Understanding the specific roles of chemokine receptors (CCR7, CCR5, CXCR3, CXCR4, CCR2) in leukocyte trafficking is crucial. The vignette highlights how the absence of a specific receptor (CCR5) on T cells can impair their migration to a site of infection (lungs in PJP), contributing to immunodeficiency. Key differentials include defects in other chemokine receptors or their ligands, or defects in T cell activation/differentiation.
