<a id='question-143'></a>

### Question 143: Cell-Mediated Immunity: Part 3 Focus 43 (expresses)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the role of specific cell surface markers (CD4, CD8, CD28, CTLA-4, PD-1) in regulating T cell activation, differentiation, and function, particularly in the context of immune tolerance and T cell exhaustion, and how these markers are differentially expressed or modulated in various T cell subsets. (理解特定细胞表面标志物（CD4、CD8、CD28、CTLA-4、PD-1）在调节T细胞活化、分化和功能中的作用，特别是在免疫耐受和T细胞耗竭的背景下，以及这些标志物如何在不同的T细胞亚群中被差异表达或调节。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with a history of chronic hepatitis C virus (HCV) infection and intravenous drug use presents with progressive fatigue, weight loss, and jaundice. He was diagnosed with hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC) 3 years ago and underwent liver transplantation 1 year ago. He is currently on immunosuppressive therapy consisting of tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, and prednisone to prevent graft rejection. Despite treatment, his HCC has recurred. During evaluation, a biopsy of the recurrent HCC reveals a dense infiltration of lymphocytes. Flow cytometry analysis of the tumor-infiltrating lymphocytes (TILs) shows a predominance of CD8+ T cells that co-express high levels of PD-1 and low levels of CD28. These CD8+ T cells exhibit reduced proliferation and cytokine (IFN-γ, TNF-α) production upon stimulation with anti-CD3/anti-CD28 antibodies compared to CD8+ T cells from healthy donors. Furthermore, blocking the PD-1 pathway in vitro significantly enhances the cytotoxic activity and cytokine production of these TILs. Which of the following cell surface molecules is most likely responsible for the reduced proliferation and cytokine production observed in these tumor-infiltrating CD8+ T cells?

(A) CD4
(B) CTLA-4
(C) PD-1
(D) CD28
(E) ICOS

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient History:** 52-year-old male, chronic HCV, IV drug use, HCC recurrence post-liver transplant, immunosuppression (tacrolimus, MMF, prednisone). This history points towards chronic inflammation, potential immune dysregulation, and a context where tumor immune surveillance might be impaired.
*   **Clinical Presentation:** Fatigue, weight loss, jaundice, recurrent HCC. Suggests ongoing disease progression and potentially immune-related complications.
*   **Tumor Microenvironment:** Dense lymphocytic infiltration in HCC biopsy. Indicates an attempt by the immune system to target the tumor.
*   **Flow Cytometry:** Predominance of CD8+ T cells (cytotoxic T lymphocytes, CTLs) within the TILs. These are the primary effector cells against tumor cells.
*   **Cell Surface Marker Expression:** High PD-1, low CD28 on tumor-infiltrating CD8+ T cells. This is a crucial finding. PD-1 is an inhibitory receptor, and CD28 is a co-stimulatory receptor. High PD-1 and low CD28 are hallmarks of T cell exhaustion, often seen in chronic infections (like HCV) and cancer.
*   **Functional Assays:** Reduced proliferation and cytokine production (IFN-γ, TNF-α) upon stimulation. This confirms functional impairment (exhaustion) of the TILs.
*   **PD-1 Blockade:** Blocking PD-1 in vitro enhances CTL activity. This demonstrates that the PD-1 pathway is indeed responsible for the observed inhibition.
*   **Question Stem:** Asks for the molecule responsible for the reduced proliferation and cytokine production.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
*   **T Cell Activation:** Naive T cells require two signals for activation: Signal 1 (TCR engagement with MHC-peptide complex) and Signal 2 (co-stimulation, primarily via CD28 binding to B7 molecules on antigen-presenting cells).
*   **T Cell Exhaustion:** Chronic antigen stimulation (e.g., in chronic viral infections or cancer) can lead to T cell exhaustion. Exhausted T cells are characterized by:
    *   Progressive loss of effector functions (cytokine production, cytotoxicity).
    *   Impaired proliferation.
    *   Upregulation of inhibitory receptors (e.g., PD-1, CTLA-4, TIM-3, LAG-3).
    *   Downregulation of co-stimulatory receptors (e.g., CD28).
    *   Epigenetic changes leading to a stable, dysfunctional state.
*   **PD-1 Pathway:** PD-1 (Programmed cell death protein 1) is an inhibitory receptor expressed on activated T cells, B cells, NK cells, and monocytes. Its ligands are PD-L1 (B7-H1) and PD-L2 (B7-DC), which are often upregulated on tumor cells, antigen-presenting cells, and other cells in the tumor microenvironment. Engagement of PD-1 by its ligands delivers an inhibitory signal that dampens T cell activation, proliferation, and effector function, preventing excessive immune responses and autoimmunity. In the context of cancer, PD-1 upregulation on TILs contributes to immune evasion by the tumor. Blocking the PD-1/PD-L1 pathway is a successful cancer immunotherapy strategy.
*   **CD28 Pathway:** CD28 is a co-stimulatory receptor expressed on most T cells. It binds to B7-1 (CD80) and B7-2 (CD86) on APCs. CD28 signaling is crucial for T cell activation, survival, and proliferation. Chronic T cell activation can lead to downregulation of CD28, contributing to T cell dysfunction and exhaustion.
*   **CTLA-4 Pathway:** CTLA-4 (Cytotoxic T-lymphocyte-associated protein 4) is another inhibitory receptor, primarily expressed on T regulatory cells (Tregs) and activated T cells. It also binds to B7 molecules (CD80/CD86) but with higher affinity than CD28. CTLA-4 competes with CD28 for B7 binding, thereby inhibiting T cell activation. It also actively removes B7 molecules from the surface of APCs. CTLA-4 blockade is another successful cancer immunotherapy strategy, particularly effective in melanoma.
*   **ICOS Pathway:** ICOS (Inducible T-cell costimulator) is a co-stimulatory receptor expressed on activated T cells, particularly T helper cells. It binds to ICOS ligand (ICOSL) on APCs and other cells. ICOS signaling promotes T cell proliferation, survival, and cytokine production, particularly IL-2 and IL-10. It plays a role in T cell help for B cells and T regulatory cell function.

In this vignette, the TILs are CD8+ T cells exhibiting signs of exhaustion (high PD-1, low CD28, reduced function). Blocking PD-1 restores function, directly implicating the PD-1 pathway as the primary inhibitory mechanism responsible for the observed dysfunction.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **CD4:** CD4 is a co-receptor for the T cell receptor (TCR) primarily expressed on helper T cells (Th cells). It binds to MHC class II molecules. While CD4+ T cells are important in anti-tumor immunity (e.g., helping CD8+ T cells), the TILs described are predominantly CD8+ T cells, and CD4 itself is not an inhibitory receptor directly responsible for T cell exhaustion in this context. CD4 is essential for TCR signaling but not the specific molecule causing the *reduced* function described.
- (B) **CTLA-4:** CTLA-4 is an inhibitory receptor that competes with CD28 for B7 binding, primarily acting early in T cell activation. While CTLA-4 contributes to immune regulation and can be upregulated on exhausted T cells, the vignette specifically highlights high PD-1 and low CD28 expression, and the functional rescue occurs upon PD-1 blockade. PD-1 is generally considered a more prominent negative regulator of effector function in chronically stimulated T cells within the tumor microenvironment compared to CTLA-4, although both can be involved. The key clue is the functional rescue by blocking PD-1.
- (D) **CD28:** CD28 is a *co-stimulatory* receptor essential for T cell activation and proliferation. The vignette states that these exhausted T cells have *low* levels of CD28. Therefore, CD28 deficiency contributes to the reduced function, but the question asks for the molecule *responsible* for the reduced function, and the functional rescue points directly to the inhibitory pathway (PD-1). CD28 downregulation is a *marker* of exhaustion, not the primary *inhibitory* molecule causing the dysfunction in this specific scenario where PD-1 blockade works.
- (E) **ICOS:** ICOS is a *co-stimulatory* receptor involved in T cell help and survival. It is not typically associated with T cell exhaustion or inhibition in the way PD-1 or CTLA-4 are. Its role is generally pro-inflammatory and supportive of T cell responses.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature           | PD-1                                   | CTLA-4                                  | CD28                                   | CD4                                     | ICOS                                   |
| :---------------- | :------------------------------------- | :-------------------------------------- | :------------------------------------- | :-------------------------------------- | :------------------------------------- |
| **Function**      | Inhibitory                             | Inhibitory                              | Co-stimulatory                         | Co-receptor (MHC II)                    | Co-stimulatory                         |
| **Expression**    | Activated T cells, exhausted T cells   | Tregs, activated T cells                | Naive & activated T cells              | Helper T cells                          | Activated T cells                      |
| **Ligands**       | PD-L1 (B7-H1), PD-L2 (B7-DC)           | B7-1 (CD80), B7-2 (CD86)                | B7-1 (CD80), B7-2 (CD86)                | MHC Class II                            | ICOS Ligand (ICOSL)                    |
| **Mechanism**     | Inhibits TCR signaling, reduces cytokine | Competes with CD28 for B7, removes B7   | Enhances TCR signaling, survival       | Binds MHC II, TCR co-receptor           | Enhances TCR signaling, cytokine prod. |
| **Role in Exhaustion** | Key inhibitory receptor in exhaustion | Contributes to regulation/exhaustion     | Downregulated in exhaustion            | Not directly inhibitory in exhaustion   | Not directly inhibitory in exhaustion   |
| **Therapeutic Target** | Yes (e.g., Pembrolizumab, Nivolumab) | Yes (e.g., Ipilimumab)                   | No                                     | No                                      | No                                     |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
T cell exhaustion is a state of dysfunction characterized by the upregulation of inhibitory receptors (like PD-1 and CTLA-4) and downregulation of co-stimulatory receptors (like CD28) on chronically stimulated T cells. PD-1 signaling inhibits T cell effector function. Blocking PD-1 can restore T cell activity and is a key cancer immunotherapy strategy. Differentiating between co-stimulatory (CD28, ICOS) and inhibitory (PD-1, CTLA-4) receptors and understanding their roles in T cell activation and exhaustion is crucial.

---

<a id='question-144'></a>

### Question 144: Cell-Mediated Immunity: Part 3 Focus 44 (receptor)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the role of specific cell surface receptors (e.g., CD4, CD8, TCR, MHC) in T cell activation, differentiation, and function, particularly in the context of graft rejection and distinguishing between different types of T cells and their target recognition. (理解特定细胞表面受体（例如 CD4、CD8、TCR、MHC）在 T 细胞活化、分化和功能中的作用，特别是在移植物排斥的背景下，以及区分不同类型的 T 细胞及其靶标识别。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with a history of chronic kidney disease receives a kidney transplant from a deceased donor. He is started on standard immunosuppressive therapy, including cyclosporine, mycophenolate mofetil, and prednisone. Three months post-transplant, he develops fever, malaise, and worsening renal function. A renal biopsy reveals acute tubular necrosis and interstitial inflammation with lymphocytic infiltrates. Flow cytometry analysis of the lymphocytic infiltrates shows a predominance of CD8+ T cells expressing cytotoxic granules (perforin and granzymes) and a significant number of CD4+ T cells. The patient's serum creatinine rises sharply, and he requires initiation of hemodialysis. Further investigation reveals that the donor kidney expresses HLA-A*02:01 and HLA-B*07:02 alleles, while the recipient expresses HLA-A*01:01 and HLA-B*08:01 alleles. Which of the following cell surface receptors is MOST directly involved in the recognition of the donor kidney cells by the recipient's cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs)?

(A) CD4
(B) CD28
(C) CD8
(D) MHC class II
(E) TCR

#### Correct Answer: (E)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male, kidney transplant recipient. This sets the stage for potential graft rejection.
- **Immunosuppression:** Standard immunosuppressive therapy (cyclosporine, mycophenolate, prednisone) is used to prevent rejection, but it can fail.
- **Symptoms:** Fever, malaise, worsening renal function 3 months post-transplant suggest acute rejection.
- **Biopsy Findings:** Acute tubular necrosis, interstitial inflammation with lymphocytic infiltrates confirm acute rejection.
- **Flow Cytometry:** Predominance of CD8+ T cells (CTLs) expressing cytotoxic granules (perforin, granzymes) indicates cell-mediated rejection, specifically cytotoxic T lymphocyte-mediated rejection. CD4+ T cells are also present, likely helper T cells contributing to the inflammatory response.
- **HLA Mismatch:** Donor (HLA-A*02:01, HLA-B*07:02) vs. Recipient (HLA-A*01:01, HLA-B*08:01) shows significant HLA mismatch, a major trigger for T cell-mediated rejection.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the receptor MOST directly involved in the recognition of donor kidney cells by the recipient's CTLs (CD8+ T cells).

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **T Cell Activation & Recognition:** T cell activation requires two signals. Signal 1 involves the T cell receptor (TCR) recognizing a peptide antigen presented by an MHC molecule on an antigen-presenting cell (APC) or target cell. Signal 2 involves co-stimulatory molecules (e.g., CD28 on T cell binding to B7 on APC).
- **CTL Recognition:** Cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs) are CD8+ T cells. Their TCR recognizes peptide antigens presented by MHC class I molecules. In the context of graft rejection, recipient CTLs recognize foreign MHC class I molecules (from the donor kidney cells) presenting peptides (either donor peptides or self-peptides presented by foreign MHC).
- **Helper T Cell Recognition:** Helper T cells (Th cells) are CD4+ T cells. Their TCR recognizes peptide antigens presented by MHC class II molecules, typically on APCs.
- **Receptor Roles:**
    - **TCR (T Cell Receptor):** The primary receptor responsible for antigen specificity. It binds to the peptide-MHC complex.
    - **CD8:** A co-receptor found on CTLs. It binds to the MHC class I molecule, stabilizing the interaction between the TCR and the peptide-MHC complex, and enhancing T cell activation.
    - **CD4:** A co-receptor found on helper T cells. It binds to the MHC class II molecule, stabilizing the interaction between the TCR and the peptide-MHC complex, and enhancing T cell activation.
    - **CD28:** A co-stimulatory receptor on T cells that binds to B7 molecules (CD80/CD86) on APCs, providing the second signal for T cell activation.
    - **MHC class II:** Presents antigens to CD4+ T cells. Not directly involved in CTL recognition.
- **Graft Rejection Mechanism:** In this case, the recipient's CD8+ CTLs recognize the donor's MHC class I molecules (HLA-A*02:01, HLA-B*07:02) as foreign. The TCR on the CTL binds to the peptide presented by the foreign MHC class I, and CD8 binds to the MHC class I molecule itself, leading to CTL activation and killing of the donor kidney cells.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **CD4:** This is the co-receptor for helper T cells (CD4+ T cells), which recognize antigens presented by MHC class II molecules. While CD4+ T cells are involved in the rejection process (e.g., as helper cells), they are not the primary effector cells (CTLs) recognizing the graft cells via MHC class I, and CD4 is not the receptor directly involved in CTL recognition of MHC class I.
- (B) **CD28:** This is a co-stimulatory receptor crucial for T cell activation (Signal 2). While necessary for a full T cell response, it is not the primary receptor responsible for antigen recognition (Signal 1). The TCR is the primary antigen-specific receptor.
- (C) **CD8:** This is a co-receptor found on CTLs (CD8+ T cells). It binds to MHC class I molecules, stabilizing the TCR-MHC interaction. While essential for CTL function and recognition, the TCR is the molecule that directly binds the peptide-MHC complex and determines antigen specificity. The question asks for the receptor MOST directly involved in *recognition*, which is the TCR. CD8 enhances the interaction but doesn't directly bind the peptide-MHC complex.
- (D) **MHC class II:** These molecules present antigens to CD4+ T cells. CTLs (CD8+ T cells) recognize antigens presented by MHC class I molecules. Therefore, MHC class II is not involved in the recognition of the graft by the recipient's CTLs.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature          | TCR (Correct Answer)                                  | CD8                                       | CD4                                       | CD28                                     | MHC Class II                               |
|-------------------|-------------------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------|
| **Cell Type**     | All T cells (CD4+, CD8+)                             | CD8+ T cells (CTLs)                        | CD4+ T cells (Helper T cells)              | All T cells                             | APCs, B cells, Thymic Epithelial Cells   |
| **Ligand**        | Peptide-MHC complex                                  | MHC Class I                               | MHC Class II                              | B7 (CD80/CD86)                           | Peptide                                   |
| **Function**      | Antigen recognition (Signal 1)                        | Co-receptor, stabilizes TCR-MHC I binding | Co-receptor, stabilizes TCR-MHC II binding | Co-stimulation (Signal 2)                | Antigen presentation to CD4+ T cells      |
| **Relevance Here** | Directly recognizes peptide presented by donor MHC I | Stabilizes TCR-MHC I interaction          | Not directly involved in CTL recognition  | Not directly involved in antigen recognition | Presents antigens to CD4+ T cells, not CTLs |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The T cell receptor (TCR) is the primary molecule responsible for recognizing specific peptide antigens presented by MHC molecules. CD8+ CTLs recognize peptides presented by MHC class I, while CD4+ helper T cells recognize peptides presented by MHC class II. CD4 and CD8 are co-receptors that enhance T cell activation by binding to the MHC molecule itself. CD28 is a co-stimulatory molecule required for full T cell activation. In graft rejection, recipient T cells recognize foreign MHC molecules on the donor tissue. Key differentials include understanding the roles of different T cell subsets (CD4 vs. CD8), MHC classes (I vs. II), and the distinction between antigen recognition (TCR) and co-stimulation/co-reception (CD4, CD8, CD28).

<a id='question-145'></a>

### Question 145: Cell-Mediated Immunity: Part 3 Focus 45 (molecules)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the role of specific cell surface molecules (CD4, CD8, MHC class I/II, TCR) in T cell activation, differentiation, and effector function, particularly in the context of graft rejection. (理解特定细胞表面分子（CD4、CD8、MHC I/II 类、TCR）在 T 细胞活化、分化和效应功能中的作用，特别是在移植物排斥的背景下。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with a history of chronic kidney disease receives a kidney transplant from a deceased donor with an unknown HLA type. He is started on standard immunosuppressive therapy including cyclosporine, mycophenolate mofetil, and prednisone. Three weeks post-transplant, he develops fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, and elevated serum creatinine. A renal biopsy reveals acute tubular necrosis and interstitial inflammation with infiltration of mononuclear cells. Immunofluorescence staining shows deposition of C4d in the peritubular capillaries. Flow cytometry analysis of the peripheral blood shows a significant decrease in the percentage of CD8+ T cells expressing CD28. The patient's serum contains antibodies against donor HLA antigens.

Which of the following molecules is most likely deficient on the surface of the patient's CD8+ T cells, contributing to the acute rejection episode?

(A) CD3
(B) CD4
(C) CD25 (IL-2 receptor alpha chain)
(D) CD28
(E) CD45

#### Correct Answer: (D)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male, chronic kidney disease, kidney transplant recipient. This sets the stage for potential graft rejection.
- **Immunosuppression:** Standard therapy (cyclosporine, mycophenolate, prednisone) is used to prevent rejection, but rejection can still occur.
- **Symptoms:** Fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, elevated creatinine three weeks post-transplant are classic signs of acute graft rejection, specifically acute cellular rejection (ACR) or antibody-mediated rejection (AMR).
- **Biopsy Findings:** Acute tubular necrosis, interstitial inflammation with mononuclear cell infiltration, and C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries strongly suggest acute antibody-mediated rejection (AMR), often referred to as acute rejection with C4d deposition. C4d is a breakdown product of the complement C4 component, indicating complement activation.
- **Flow Cytometry:** Significant decrease in CD8+ T cells expressing CD28 is a crucial finding. CD28 is a co-stimulatory molecule essential for T cell activation. Its absence or deficiency leads to impaired T cell function.
- **Antibodies:** Presence of donor HLA antibodies confirms an antibody-mediated component to the rejection.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the specific molecule deficient on CD8+ T cells contributing to the rejection. The combination of AMR (C4d deposition, donor HLA antibodies) and decreased CD28 expression on CD8+ T cells points towards a specific immunological defect.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **T Cell Activation:** Naive T cells require two signals for full activation: Signal 1 (TCR engagement with MHC-peptide complex) and Signal 2 (co-stimulation, primarily CD28 on T cells binding to B7 molecules (CD80/CD86) on antigen-presenting cells (APCs)).
- **CD28 Function:** CD28 is a crucial co-stimulatory molecule expressed on most T cells (both CD4+ and CD8+). It binds to CD80 (B7-1) and CD86 (B7-2) on APCs. This interaction provides the essential "second signal" for T cell activation, proliferation, cytokine production, and survival.
- **CD8+ T Cells:** Cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs) are primarily CD8+ T cells. They recognize antigens presented on MHC class I molecules and are crucial for eliminating infected or cancerous cells. They also play a role in graft rejection by directly killing graft cells.
- **Graft Rejection:** Acute cellular rejection (ACR) involves T cell-mediated cytotoxicity against graft cells. Antibody-mediated rejection (AMR) involves donor-specific antibodies (DSAs) binding to graft endothelium, activating complement, and recruiting inflammatory cells. The presence of C4d indicates complement activation, characteristic of AMR.
- **CD28 Deficiency:** Deficiency or absence of CD28 on T cells leads to impaired T cell activation and function. This can result from genetic defects (e.g., mutations in the *CD28* gene) or acquired conditions. Patients with CD28 deficiency are susceptible to severe infections, particularly with intracellular pathogens, and may have altered responses to transplantation.
- **Link to Vignette:** The patient is experiencing acute rejection (likely a mixed cellular and humoral rejection given the C4d deposition and donor HLA antibodies). The flow cytometry finding of decreased CD28 expression on CD8+ T cells suggests that these cells are functionally impaired, potentially contributing to the rejection process or representing an underlying immune dysregulation. While CD28 deficiency typically leads to immunodeficiency, in the context of transplantation, impaired T cell function could paradoxically contribute to rejection by altering the balance of immune responses or potentially leading to the development of DSAs. The question specifically asks for the molecule deficient on CD8+ T cells, which is directly stated in the flow cytometry results.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **CD3:** CD3 is part of the T cell receptor (TCR) complex and is essential for signal transduction following TCR engagement (Signal 1). Deficiency leads to severe combined immunodeficiency (SCID). While crucial for T cell function, the vignette specifically points to a defect in co-stimulation, not the primary signaling pathway.
- (B) **CD4:** CD4 is a co-receptor expressed primarily on helper T cells (Th cells). It binds to MHC class II molecules. While CD4+ T cells play a role in orchestrating immune responses, including graft rejection, the flow cytometry specifically identified a deficiency in CD28 expression on *CD8+* T cells, not CD4+ T cells.
- (C) **CD25 (IL-2 receptor alpha chain):** CD25 is the alpha chain of the high-affinity IL-2 receptor, expressed on activated T cells and regulatory T cells (Tregs). It is important for T cell proliferation and survival. While IL-2 signaling is crucial, the specific finding in the vignette is related to CD28 deficiency, which affects the initial co-stimulatory signal required for T cell activation.
- (E) **CD45:** CD45 is a protein tyrosine phosphatase expressed on all hematopoietic cells except erythrocytes and platelets. It is essential for T cell receptor signaling. Deficiency leads to severe immunodeficiency. Like CD3, it's involved in the primary signaling pathway, not the co-stimulatory pathway affected by CD28.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature             | CD3 Deficiency (SCID) | CD4 Deficiency (HIV) | CD25 Deficiency (Rare) | CD28 Deficiency (Immunodeficiency) | CD45 Deficiency (SCID) |
|----------------------|-----------------------|-----------------------|------------------------|-----------------------------------|-----------------------|
| **Cell Type Affected** | All T cells           | CD4+ T cells          | Activated T cells, Tregs | Most T cells (CD4+ & CD8+)       | All leukocytes       |
| **Primary Function** | TCR Signaling         | Helper Function       | IL-2 Signaling         | Co-stimulation                    | TCR Signaling         |
| **Key Molecule**      | CD3 complex           | CD4                  | CD25                   | CD28                              | CD45                  |
| **Clinical Manifestation** | Severe Immunodeficiency | Opportunistic Infections, AIDS | Variable, potential immune dysregulation | Severe Infections, altered transplant response | Severe Immunodeficiency |
| **Relevance to Vignette** | No                   | No                   | No                    | **Yes (Directly mentioned)**      | No                   |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The vignette highlights the importance of co-stimulation (CD28-B7 interaction) for T cell activation and function, particularly CD8+ T cells in the context of graft rejection. Understanding the roles of different cell surface molecules (CD3, CD4, CD8, CD28, CD45) and their involvement in T cell signaling and activation is crucial. Differential diagnoses for graft rejection include acute cellular rejection (T cell mediated) and antibody-mediated rejection (humoral). Key immunological markers like C4d deposition and flow cytometry analysis of T cell subsets are essential for diagnosis and management.

<a id='question-146'></a>

### Question 146: Cell-Mediated Immunity: Part 3 Focus 46 (factors.)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the role of specific cytokines (like IL-2) and their receptors (like CD25, the IL-2 receptor alpha chain) in T cell proliferation, differentiation, and function, particularly in the context of immune regulation and potential therapeutic targeting. (理解特定细胞因子（如IL-2）及其受体（如CD25，IL-2受体α链）在T细胞增殖、分化和功能中的作用，特别是在免疫调节和潜在治疗靶点方面。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 45-year-old male patient with a history of acute myeloid leukemia (AML) undergoes allogeneic hematopoietic stem cell transplantation (HSCT) from an HLA-matched unrelated donor. His conditioning regimen includes fludarabine and busulfan. Post-transplant, he develops severe graft-versus-host disease (GVHD) affecting the skin, liver, and gastrointestinal tract. Standard immunosuppressive therapy with cyclosporine and methotrexate fails to control the GVHD. As part of a clinical trial, he is treated with an experimental monoclonal antibody targeting the alpha chain (CD25) of the IL-2 receptor. Following this treatment, his GVHD symptoms significantly improve, and his liver function tests normalize. However, three months later, he develops recurrent, aggressive cytomegalovirus (CMV) infection, which is refractory to ganciclovir therapy. Flow cytometry analysis of his peripheral blood lymphocytes reveals a marked reduction in the number of CD8+ T cells expressing CD25.

Which of the following mechanisms BEST explains the patient's increased susceptibility to CMV infection after treatment with the anti-CD25 monoclonal antibody?

(A) The antibody directly inhibited CMV replication by binding to viral surface glycoproteins.
(B) The antibody enhanced the activity of regulatory T cells (Tregs), leading to suppression of the anti-CMV immune response.
(C) The antibody depleted CD4+ T cells, which are crucial for orchestrating the immune response against CMV.
(D) The antibody impaired the proliferation and effector function of activated CD8+ T cells, which are essential for controlling CMV infection.
(E) The antibody induced the production of neutralizing antibodies against CMV, leading to antibody-dependent enhancement (ADE) of viral entry into host cells.

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Profile:** 45-year-old male with AML undergoing allogeneic HSCT. This sets the stage for potential GVHD and immunosuppression.
*   **Conditioning Regimen:** Fludarabine and busulfan are highly immunosuppressive, increasing the risk of opportunistic infections like CMV.
*   **Complication:** Severe GVHD refractory to standard therapy. This indicates a need for more potent immunosuppression.
*   **Experimental Therapy:** Monoclonal antibody targeting CD25 (IL-2 receptor alpha chain). CD25 is highly expressed on activated T cells and regulatory T cells (Tregs).
*   **Outcome of Therapy:** Improvement in GVHD symptoms. This suggests the antibody successfully suppressed the alloreactive T cells causing GVHD.
*   **Subsequent Complication:** Recurrent, aggressive, ganciclovir-refractory CMV infection. This points to impaired cell-mediated immunity against CMV.
*   **Immunophenotyping:** Marked reduction in CD8+ T cells expressing CD25. This is a crucial clue linking the therapy to the subsequent infection.
*   **Question Stem:** Asks for the mechanism explaining increased susceptibility to CMV infection after anti-CD25 therapy.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
*   **IL-2 and CD25:** Interleukin-2 (IL-2) is a critical cytokine for T cell proliferation, differentiation, and survival. It binds to the IL-2 receptor (IL-2R), which consists of three subunits: CD25 (alpha chain), CD122 (beta chain), and CD132 (gamma chain). The high-affinity IL-2R (CD25βγ) is expressed primarily on activated T cells, Tregs, and some B cells.
*   **CD25 Function:** CD25 expression is upregulated on T cells upon activation (e.g., by antigen presentation and co-stimulation). Binding of IL-2 to the CD25βγ receptor triggers intracellular signaling pathways (JAK-STAT) that promote T cell clonal expansion and differentiation into effector and memory cells.
*   **Role in GVHD:** Alloreactive T cells from the donor graft recognize host antigens as foreign, leading to GVHD. These T cells are activated and express high levels of CD25, making them highly responsive to IL-2. Blocking CD25 with a monoclonal antibody inhibits IL-2 signaling in these alloreactive T cells, thereby suppressing GVHD.
*   **Role in Anti-CMV Immunity:** Control of CMV infection relies heavily on cell-mediated immunity, particularly cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs), which are primarily CD8+ T cells. Upon encountering CMV-infected cells, CD8+ T cells become activated, proliferate, and differentiate into effector CTLs. This activation process involves IL-2 signaling through the CD25βγ receptor, which is crucial for their expansion and cytotoxic function.
*   **Effect of Anti-CD25 Antibody:** The monoclonal antibody targeting CD25 blocks IL-2 binding to its receptor. While this effectively suppresses GVHD by inhibiting alloreactive T cells, it also impairs the activation, proliferation, and effector function of *all* T cells that rely on IL-2 signaling, including the anti-CMV CD8+ T cells. The flow cytometry finding of reduced CD25 expression on CD8+ T cells is likely a consequence of reduced activation or a shift in phenotype, but the *functional* consequence is impaired IL-2 responsiveness due to the blocking antibody. The antibody prevents IL-2 from binding to CD25, thus inhibiting the downstream signaling required for T cell proliferation and effector function necessary to control CMV.
*   **CMV Pathogenesis:** CMV is a herpesvirus that establishes latent infection. Reactivation or primary infection can cause severe disease, especially in immunocompromised individuals. Cell-mediated immunity (CTLs) is the primary defense mechanism against CMV.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): The antibody targets the IL-2 receptor (CD25) on T cells, not CMV directly. It does not inhibit viral replication. This option confuses the mechanism of action of the antibody with antiviral drugs like ganciclovir.
- (B): While CD25 is expressed on Tregs, the primary function of Tregs is immune suppression. Blocking CD25 *reduces* Treg activity, which would generally *enhance* anti-viral immunity, not suppress it. The patient's susceptibility to CMV is due to impaired effector T cell function, not enhanced Treg activity.
- (C): CD4+ T cells (helper T cells) play a role in orchestrating immune responses, including activating CD8+ T cells and B cells. While their function might be somewhat affected by IL-2 blockade, the primary effector cells controlling CMV are CD8+ CTLs. The vignette specifically mentions a reduction in CD25 expression on CD8+ T cells, directly implicating their function. Impairing CD4+ T cells alone is less likely to cause such severe CMV susceptibility compared to impairing CD8+ T cell function.
- (E): Neutralizing antibodies against CMV are part of the humoral immune response. Antibody-dependent enhancement (ADE) is a phenomenon where antibodies enhance viral entry, typically seen with certain viruses like Dengue, not CMV. The patient's problem is impaired cell-mediated immunity, not an aberrant antibody response.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Correct Answer (D) - Impaired CD8+ T cell function via IL-2 blockade | Distractor (A) - Direct antiviral effect | Distractor (B) - Enhanced Treg activity | Distractor (C) - Impaired CD4+ T cell function | Distractor (E) - ADE via antibodies |
| :---------------------- | :------------------------------------------------------------------- | :-------------------------------------- | :--------------------------------------- | :------------------------------------------- | :----------------------------------- |
| **Mechanism**           | Blocks IL-2 signaling -> Impaired CD8+ T cell proliferation/function | Direct inhibition of viral replication | Enhanced Treg suppression               | Impaired CD4+ T cell help/orchestration    | Antibody-mediated viral entry enhancement |
| **Target**              | IL-2 Receptor (CD25) on T cells                                    | Viral proteins/enzymes                  | IL-2 Receptor (CD25) on Tregs            | T cell receptor/co-stimulatory molecules   | Viral surface glycoproteins          |
| **Immune Component**    | Cell-mediated (CD8+ CTLs)                                           | Antiviral drugs                         | Cell-mediated (Tregs)                    | Cell-mediated (CD4+ Th cells)               | Humoral (Antibodies)                  |
| **Clinical Relevance**  | Increased susceptibility to intracellular pathogens (e.g., CMV)       | Treatment for viral infections           | Autoimmunity/GVHD suppression            | Impaired T cell help, increased infections | Dengue, other flaviviruses             |
| **Key Cytokine/Receptor** | IL-2 / CD25                                                         | N/A                                     | IL-2 / CD25                              | N/A                                          | N/A                                  |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Blocking the IL-2 receptor (CD25) with antibodies like daclizumab or basiliximab is used to prevent acute rejection in transplant recipients and treat certain autoimmune diseases by suppressing T cell activation and proliferation. However, this also impairs cell-mediated immunity, increasing the risk of opportunistic infections, particularly those controlled by CD8+ T cells like CMV, EBV, and certain fungi/bacteria. Key differentials include other causes of immunosuppression (e.g., standard immunosuppressants, underlying immunodeficiency) and specific viral pathogens. Understanding the role of IL-2 and CD25 in T cell biology is crucial.

<a id='question-147'></a>

### Question 147: Cell-Mediated Immunity: Part 3 Focus 47 (important)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the role of different T cell subsets (Th1, Th2, Th17, Treg) and their associated cytokines in orchestrating immune responses, particularly in the context of intracellular pathogens and chronic inflammation. (理解不同 T 细胞亚群（Th1、Th2、Th17、Treg）及其相关细胞因子在协调免疫应答中的作用，特别是在胞内病原体和慢性炎症的背景下。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 32-year-old male presents to the emergency department with a 3-week history of worsening cough, fever, night sweats, and significant weight loss. He is HIV-positive with a CD4 count of 85 cells/µL. He reports no recent travel or known exposure to tuberculosis. Chest X-ray reveals bilateral diffuse interstitial infiltrates. Sputum samples are sent for analysis. Acid-fast bacilli (AFB) staining is positive. Culture of the sputum grows *Mycobacterium avium* complex (MAC). The patient is started on a multi-drug regimen including clarithromycin, ethambutol, and rifabutin. Despite treatment, his symptoms persist, and he develops severe, painful, erythematous subcutaneous nodules on his extremities. A biopsy of one of these nodules shows non-caseating granulomas with numerous AFB. Laboratory tests reveal elevated levels of IL-10 and decreased levels of IFN-γ. Which of the following immunological mechanisms is most likely contributing to the patient's persistent infection and development of cutaneous manifestations?

(A) Impaired activation of cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs) due to defective MHC class I presentation.
(B) Defective Th1 cell differentiation and cytokine production, leading to inadequate macrophage activation.
(C) Excessive Th2 cell activation, promoting antibody production against mycobacterial antigens.
(D) Increased activity of regulatory T cells (Tregs), suppressing the inflammatory response necessary for pathogen clearance.
(E) Aberrant Th17 cell response, characterized by increased production of IL-22 and recruitment of neutrophils.

#### Correct Answer: (B)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Profile:** 32-year-old male, HIV-positive, CD4 count 85 cells/µL (severe immunosuppression). This immediately points towards opportunistic infections, particularly those typically controlled by cell-mediated immunity.
*   **Symptoms:** Chronic cough, fever, night sweats, weight loss (constitutional symptoms suggestive of chronic infection, often TB or atypical mycobacteria).
*   **Imaging:** Bilateral diffuse interstitial infiltrates (consistent with disseminated infection, often seen in MAC in AIDS patients).
*   **Microbiology:** AFB-positive sputum, culture positive for *Mycobacterium avium* complex (MAC). MAC is a common opportunistic infection in patients with advanced HIV/AIDS (CD4 < 50).
*   **Clinical Course:** Persistence of symptoms despite multi-drug therapy. Development of painful, erythematous subcutaneous nodules (erythema nodosum-like lesions, a common manifestation of disseminated MAC infection).
*   **Histopathology:** Non-caseating granulomas with AFB (characteristic of MAC infection, though caseating granulomas are more typical for *M. tuberculosis*).
*   **Immunology:** Elevated IL-10, decreased IFN-γ. IL-10 is an immunosuppressive cytokine, while IFN-γ is crucial for macrophage activation and Th1 responses. This cytokine profile suggests a skewed immune response away from effective pathogen control.
*   **Question Stem:** Asks for the immunological mechanism contributing to persistent infection and cutaneous manifestations.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
*   **MAC Infection in AIDS:** MAC is an intracellular pathogen primarily controlled by cell-mediated immunity, specifically Th1 responses. IFN-γ produced by Th1 cells activates macrophages, enhancing their ability to kill intracellular mycobacteria.
*   **HIV and Cell-Mediated Immunity:** HIV directly infects and destroys CD4+ T cells, including Th1 cells. Severe CD4 depletion (as in this patient, CD4=85) leads to profound impairment of Th1 responses.
*   **Th1 Response:** Th1 cells are characterized by the production of IFN-γ, IL-2, and TNF-α. IFN-γ is the key cytokine for activating macrophages to kill intracellular pathogens like mycobacteria.
*   **Cytokine Profile:** The patient has elevated IL-10 (immunosuppressive) and decreased IFN-γ (essential for macrophage activation). This profile indicates a failure of the Th1 response.
*   **Mechanism of Persistent Infection:** The lack of a robust Th1 response (due to low CD4 count and direct HIV effect) means macrophages are not adequately activated to kill MAC. This leads to persistent intracellular infection, dissemination, and clinical manifestations like pulmonary infiltrates and cutaneous nodules.
*   **Option (B) Explanation:** Defective Th1 cell differentiation and cytokine production (specifically IFN-γ) directly impairs macrophage activation, which is the primary mechanism for controlling MAC. This aligns perfectly with the patient's severe immunosuppression (low CD4 count), the nature of the pathogen (intracellular), the clinical presentation (disseminated MAC), and the cytokine profile (low IFN-γ).

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
*   **(A) Impaired activation of cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs) due to defective MHC class I presentation.** While CTLs (CD8+ T cells) are important for controlling viral infections and some intracellular bacteria, the primary defense against MAC relies on macrophage activation mediated by Th1 cells and IFN-γ. CTLs are less critical for MAC clearance. Furthermore, MHC class I presentation is primarily involved in CTL activation against infected *host* cells, not directly in macrophage activation against intracellular pathogens like MAC. The key defect here is macrophage activation, driven by Th1/IFN-γ.
*   **(C) Excessive Th2 cell activation, promoting antibody production against mycobacterial antigens.** Th2 responses are primarily involved in defense against extracellular parasites and allergic reactions. They promote antibody production (IgE, IgG1) and eosinophil activation. Antibodies are generally ineffective against intracellular pathogens like MAC, which reside within macrophages. While some antibody response might occur, it's not the primary mechanism of control, and excessive Th2 activation would further impair the necessary Th1 response. The cytokine profile (low IFN-γ) argues against a dominant Th2 response.
*   **(D) Increased activity of regulatory T cells (Tregs), suppressing the inflammatory response necessary for pathogen clearance.** Tregs (CD4+CD25+FoxP3+) suppress excessive immune responses to maintain tolerance and prevent autoimmunity. While Tregs might be involved in modulating the response to MAC, their *increased* activity is not the primary reason for the *failure* to control the infection in this severely immunocompromised patient. The fundamental problem is the lack of effector T cells (Th1) and activated macrophages due to low CD4 counts. While Tregs might contribute to the elevated IL-10, the primary defect is the absence of the activating signal (IFN-γ).
*   **(E) Aberrant Th17 cell response, characterized by increased production of IL-22 and recruitment of neutrophils.** Th17 cells are involved in defense against extracellular bacteria and fungi, particularly at mucosal surfaces. They produce IL-17 and IL-22, which promote neutrophil recruitment and inflammation. While inflammation is present, the primary defect in controlling MAC is impaired macrophage activation, a Th1 function. Th17 responses are not the main mechanism for controlling intracellular mycobacteria.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature             | MAC Infection in AIDS (Correct) | Tuberculosis (M. tuberculosis) | Viral Infection (e.g., HIV) | Extracellular Bacterial Infection (e.g., S. aureus) |
| :------------------ | :------------------------------ | :------------------------------ | :-------------------------- | :---------------------------------------------- |
| **Primary Immune Defense** | Th1/Macrophage Activation      | Th1/Macrophage Activation      | CTLs/Antibodies            | Neutrophils/Antibodies/Complement              |
| **Key Cytokine**    | IFN-γ                           | IFN-γ                           | IL-2, IFN-γ (CTLs), IL-4/6 (B cells) | IL-17, IL-8, TNF-α                             |
| **Cell Type**       | Th1 cells, Activated Macrophages | Th1 cells, Activated Macrophages | CD8+ CTLs, CD4+ T cells    | Neutrophils, Macrophages, B cells               |
| **Granuloma Type**  | Non-caseating (often)           | Caseating (typically)           | Variable                   | Usually absent                                  |
| **HIV Impact**      | Profound Th1 deficiency        | Th1 deficiency                 | CD4+ T cell depletion      | Variable, depends on specific pathogen          |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The primary defense against intracellular pathogens like *Mycobacterium avium* complex (MAC) relies on cell-mediated immunity, specifically the activation of macrophages by Th1 cells and their signature cytokine, IFN-γ. In patients with severe immunosuppression, such as advanced HIV/AIDS with low CD4 counts, this Th1 response is impaired, leading to opportunistic infections like disseminated MAC. Key differentials include *M. tuberculosis* (often caseating granulomas, but can be non-caseating in immunocompromised hosts), other opportunistic infections (e.g., *Pneumocystis jirovecii* pneumonia, CMV retinitis), and non-infectious causes of pulmonary infiltrates. Understanding the role of Th1 cells and IFN-γ in macrophage activation is crucial for understanding the pathogenesis of MAC infection in AIDS.

<a id='question-148'></a>

### Question 148: Cell-Mediated Immunity: Part 3 Focus 48 (growth)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the role of specific cytokines (IL-2) in T cell proliferation and activation, and how defects in cytokine signaling pathways or receptor expression impact cell-mediated immunity, particularly in the context of opportunistic infections. (理解细胞因子（IL-2）在T细胞增殖和激活中的作用，以及信号通路或受体表达缺陷如何影响细胞介导的免疫，特别是在机会性感染的背景下。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 35-year-old male with a history of severe combined immunodeficiency (SCID) due to X-linked common gamma chain deficiency (X-CGD) presents with a 2-week history of progressive shortness of breath, non-productive cough, and fever. He received a hematopoietic stem cell transplant (HSCT) 6 months ago, which was initially successful, but he was subsequently taken off immunosuppression 3 months ago. He is now febrile (38.5°C), tachypneic (RR 28/min), and hypoxic (SpO2 88% on room air). Chest X-ray reveals diffuse bilateral interstitial infiltrates. Bronchoalveolar lavage (BAL) fluid analysis shows numerous Pneumocystis jirovecii cysts (Gomori methenamine silver stain positive). Flow cytometry of peripheral blood lymphocytes reveals a markedly reduced number of CD4+ T cells (25 cells/µL) and normal numbers of CD8+ T cells, B cells, and NK cells. Despite treatment with trimethoprim-sulfamethoxazole, the patient's respiratory status continues to worsen. Further investigation reveals that his T cells fail to proliferate in response to stimulation with anti-CD3 antibodies and IL-2.

Which of the following is the most likely underlying defect contributing to the patient's persistent Pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia despite appropriate antimicrobial therapy?

(A) Defective expression of the IL-2 receptor beta chain (CD122) on T cells.
(B) Impaired production of interferon-gamma (IFN-γ) by CD8+ T cells.
(C) Deficiency in the complement component C3.
(D) Reduced expression of MHC class II molecules on antigen-presenting cells.
(E) Defective phagocytosis by alveolar macrophages.

#### Correct Answer: (A)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient History:** 35-year-old male with X-CGD (a form of SCID) who underwent HSCT 6 months ago. X-CGD affects signaling through receptors that share the common gamma chain (γc), including IL-2R, IL-4R, IL-7R, IL-9R, IL-15R, and IL-21R. This leads to impaired T cell and NK cell development/function.
*   **Current Presentation:** Progressive respiratory symptoms (SOB, cough, fever) 3 months after stopping immunosuppression post-HSCT. This timing suggests a potential relapse of underlying immunodeficiency or a complication related to the HSCT or prior condition.
*   **Diagnosis:** Pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia (PJP), confirmed by BAL and staining. PJP is a classic opportunistic infection in patients with impaired cell-mediated immunity, particularly CD4+ T cell deficiency.
*   **Immunological Findings:** Markedly reduced CD4+ T cells (25 cells/µL), normal CD8+ T cells, B cells, and NK cells. This points towards a specific defect affecting CD4+ T cell function or survival, rather than a global immunodeficiency affecting all lymphocyte lineages (like the original X-CGD).
*   **Functional Assay:** T cells fail to proliferate in response to anti-CD3 (TCR stimulation) and IL-2. This is the crucial clue. Anti-CD3 stimulation activates T cells, which then require IL-2 for clonal expansion (proliferation). The failure to proliferate despite both stimulation and IL-2 suggests a defect in the IL-2 signaling pathway within the T cells.
*   **Question Stem:** Asks for the underlying defect contributing to persistent PJP despite antimicrobial therapy. The persistent infection despite treatment implies a severe impairment in the host's ability to clear the organism, pointing to a fundamental immune defect.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
*   **Pneumocystis jirovecii:** A fungus that causes PJP, primarily in immunocompromised individuals, especially those with CD4+ T cell counts < 200 cells/µL. It is cleared by cell-mediated immunity involving CD4+ T cells (Th1 response) and macrophages.
*   **IL-2 and T Cell Proliferation:** IL-2 is a critical cytokine for T cell proliferation, differentiation, and survival. It binds to the IL-2 receptor (IL-2R), which consists of three subunits: IL-2Rα (CD122), IL-2Rβ (CD122), and IL-2Rγ (CD132). The high-affinity receptor requires all three chains. IL-2Rβ (CD122) and IL-2Rγ (CD132) are shared by other cytokine receptors (e.g., IL-4R, IL-7R, IL-9R, IL-15R, IL-21R).
*   **X-CGD:** The patient's history of X-CGD indicates a defect in the common gamma chain (γc, encoded by the *IL2RG* gene), which is the IL-2Rγ (CD132) subunit. This explains his initial SCID.
*   **Post-HSCT Scenario:** HSCT aims to reconstitute the immune system. The patient's current presentation suggests a failure of T cell reconstitution or function. The specific finding of reduced CD4+ T cells and failure to proliferate in response to IL-2 points towards a defect in the IL-2 signaling pathway *within the reconstituted T cells*.
*   **Mechanism:** While the HSCT likely provided cells with functional γc chains (CD132), the patient's *own* T cells, or potentially the reconstituted T cells, might have a defect in the IL-2Rβ chain (CD122). CD122 is essential for IL-2 binding and signaling. If T cells lack or have defective CD122, they cannot respond to IL-2, even if IL-2 is present and the cells are stimulated via the TCR (anti-CD3). This leads to impaired T cell proliferation and function, predisposing the patient to severe opportunistic infections like PJP. This specific defect is known as IL-2 inducible T-cell kinase deficiency (IL2ITKD) or STAT5B deficiency, which can present similarly to X-CGD but is distinct. However, given the options, the most plausible explanation for failure to respond to IL-2 stimulation is a defect in the IL-2 receptor itself, specifically the β chain (CD122).

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
*   **(B) Impaired production of interferon-gamma (IFN-γ) by CD8+ T cells:** IFN-γ is crucial for macrophage activation and defense against intracellular pathogens like *Pneumocystis*. While impaired IFN-γ production (e.g., in STAT1 deficiency) can cause susceptibility to PJP, the key finding here is the *failure to proliferate in response to IL-2*. This points directly to a problem with IL-2 signaling, not necessarily IFN-γ production by CD8+ cells (although CD4+ Th1 cells are the primary IFN-γ producers relevant to PJP).
*   **(C) Deficiency in the complement component C3:** C3 deficiency leads to impaired opsonization and increased susceptibility to encapsulated bacteria. It does not directly affect cell-mediated immunity or the clearance of *Pneumocystis*.
*   **(D) Reduced expression of MHC class II molecules on antigen-presenting cells:** MHC class II deficiency (e.g., in MHC class II deficiency syndrome) impairs CD4+ T cell activation because APCs cannot present antigens effectively. This would lead to reduced CD4+ T cell numbers and function, but it doesn't explain the specific failure of T cells to proliferate *in response to IL-2* after TCR stimulation. The problem lies within the T cell's response to the cytokine, not the initial antigen presentation.
*   **(E) Defective phagocytosis by alveolar macrophages:** While macrophages are involved in clearing *Pneumocystis*, defective phagocytosis (e.g., in Chronic Granulomatous Disease - CGD) primarily increases susceptibility to catalase-positive bacteria and fungi. Furthermore, the primary defect in clearing *Pneumocystis* is cell-mediated immunity involving T cells and activated macrophages, not just macrophage phagocytosis itself. The vignette strongly points to a T cell defect.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                     | Correct Answer (A) - Defective IL-2Rβ (CD122) | (B) Impaired IFN-γ production | (C) C3 Deficiency | (D) MHC Class II Deficiency | (E) Defective Macrophage Phagocytosis (e.g., CGD) |
| :-------------------------- | :-------------------------------------------- | :----------------------------- | :--------------- | :-------------------------- | :------------------------------------------------ |
| **Primary Immune Defect**   | T cell signaling (IL-2 response)               | T cell cytokine production     | Complement       | Antigen Presentation       | Phagocyte function                             |
| **Affected Cell Type**      | T cells                                       | CD8+ T cells (primarily)        | N/A              | APCs, CD4+ T cells          | Macrophages, Neutrophils                        |
| **Key Cytokine/Molecule**   | IL-2, IL-2Rβ (CD122)                          | IFN-γ                          | C3               | MHC Class II                | NADPH oxidase                                     |
| **Typical Infections**       | Opportunistic (PJP, CMV, etc.)                | PJP, Mycobacteria              | Encapsulated bacteria | Opportunistic (PJP, CMV, etc.) | Catalase-positive bacteria, fungi                |
| **Mechanism of PJP Suscept.** | Impaired T cell proliferation/activation     | Impaired macrophage activation | N/A              | Impaired CD4+ T cell help    | Impaired killing of ingested microbes             |
| **Vignette Clue**           | Failure to proliferate in response to IL-2    | N/A                            | N/A              | N/A                        | N/A                                               |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The failure of T cells to proliferate in response to IL-2 stimulation (after TCR activation) strongly indicates a defect in the IL-2 signaling pathway. The most common defect is in the IL-2 receptor itself, specifically the IL-2Rβ chain (CD122). This contrasts with defects in IL-2 production (rare) or downstream signaling molecules like JAKs or STATs. Remember that IL-2 is crucial for T cell clonal expansion, and its deficiency leads to severe immunodeficiency, particularly affecting CD4+ T cells and increasing susceptibility to opportunistic infections like PJP. Differentiate this from defects in IFN-γ production (affecting macrophage activation) or MHC class II expression (affecting initial T cell activation).

<a id='question-149'></a>

### Question 149: Cell-Mediated Immunity: Part 3 Focus 49 (factor)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the role of CD8+ cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs) in recognizing and eliminating virally infected cells, specifically focusing on the presentation of viral antigens via MHC class I molecules and the subsequent cytotoxic mechanisms employed by CTLs. (理解CD8+细胞毒性T淋巴细胞(CTLs)在识别和清除病毒感染细胞中的作用，特别是病毒抗原通过MHC I类分子呈递以及CTLs随后采用的细胞毒性机制。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 32-year-old male, recently diagnosed with HIV infection (CD4 count 250 cells/µL), presents with a 3-week history of worsening fatigue, fever, and a diffuse maculopapular rash. He reports no recent travel or known exposures other than unprotected sex. Physical examination reveals generalized lymphadenopathy and oral thrush. A skin biopsy from a representative lesion shows epidermal keratinocytes with large, multinucleated cells containing intranuclear inclusions. These cells also exhibit prominent expression of MHC class I molecules on their surface. Laboratory tests confirm the presence of a novel herpesvirus. The patient's condition continues to deteriorate despite initiation of highly active antiretroviral therapy (HAART). Which of the following cellular mechanisms is MOST likely responsible for the observed cytopathic effects in the skin lesions?

(A) Antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) mediated by NK cells recognizing Fc receptors on infected keratinocytes.
(B) Complement-mediated lysis of infected keratinocytes due to deposition of C3b on their surface.
(C) Cytotoxic T lymphocyte (CTL)-mediated killing of infected keratinocytes presenting viral antigens via MHC class I molecules.
(D) Macrophage phagocytosis of infected keratinocytes opsonized by antibodies and complement.
(E) Neutrophil-mediated extracellular trap (NET) formation surrounding infected keratinocytes, leading to tissue damage.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 32-year-old male with HIV (CD4 count 250 cells/µL), indicating significant immunosuppression.
- **Symptoms:** Fatigue, fever, maculopapular rash, lymphadenopathy, oral thrush – consistent with opportunistic infections and systemic viral illness in an immunocompromised host.
- **Skin Biopsy Findings:** Epidermal keratinocytes with large, multinucleated cells containing intranuclear inclusions. This morphology is characteristic of herpesvirus infections (e.g., HSV, VZV, CMV, EBV). The prominent expression of MHC class I molecules on these infected cells is a crucial clue.
- **Diagnosis:** Confirmed novel herpesvirus infection.
- **Clinical Course:** Deterioration despite HAART suggests a significant viral load and immune response, or potentially a virus less sensitive to HAART initially.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the mechanism responsible for the cytopathic effects (cell damage/death) in the skin lesions.

The key elements pointing towards CTL-mediated killing are:
1.  **Viral Infection:** Herpesviruses infect cells, often causing cytopathic effects.
2.  **MHC Class I Upregulation:** Infected cells typically upregulate MHC class I molecules to present viral antigens (peptides) on their surface. This is a strategy to alert CTLs.
3.  **Immunocompromised Host (HIV):** While the patient is immunocompromised, CTL function (CD8+ T cells) is often relatively preserved compared to CD4+ T helper cells in early HIV infection. CTLs are crucial for controlling viral infections.
4.  **Cytopathic Effects:** The description of cell damage and multinucleated cells with inclusions indicates direct cell killing.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Viral Infection & MHC Class I:** Viruses replicate inside host cells. Intracellular viral proteins are processed into peptides by the proteasome. These peptides are transported into the endoplasmic reticulum (ER), loaded onto MHC class I molecules, and transported to the cell surface. This process allows infected cells to display viral antigens. (病毒在宿主细胞内复制。细胞内病毒蛋白被蛋白酶体处理成肽段。这些肽段被转运到内质网(ER)，加载到MHC I类分子上，并转运到细胞表面。这个过程允许被感染的细胞展示病毒抗原。)
- **CD8+ Cytotoxic T Lymphocytes (CTLs):** CTLs recognize viral peptides presented by MHC class I molecules on the surface of infected cells via their T cell receptor (TCR). This interaction, along with co-stimulatory signals (e.g., CD28-B7), activates the CTL. (CD8+细胞毒性T淋巴细胞(CTLs)通过其T细胞受体(TCR)识别MHC I类分子表面呈递的病毒肽段。这种相互作用以及共刺激信号(例如CD28-B7)激活CTL。)
- **CTL Killing Mechanisms:** Activated CTLs kill target cells primarily through two mechanisms:
    1.  **Perforin/Granzyme Pathway:** CTLs release perforin (forms pores in the target cell membrane) and granzymes (serine proteases that enter the target cell via the pores and activate caspases, leading to apoptosis). (穿孔素/颗粒酶途径：CTLs释放穿孔素(在靶细胞膜上形成孔洞)和颗粒酶(丝氨酸蛋白酶，通过孔洞进入靶细胞并激活caspases，导致凋亡)。)
    2.  **Fas/FasL Pathway:** CTLs express Fas ligand (FasL) on their surface, which binds to Fas (CD95) receptors on the target cell surface, triggering apoptosis. (Fas/FasL途径：CTLs在其表面表达Fas配体(FasL)，与靶细胞表面的Fas(CD95)受体结合，触发凋亡。)
- **Role in Herpesvirus Infection:** CTLs are critical for controlling herpesvirus infections by eliminating infected cells that present viral antigens via MHC class I. The cytopathic effects observed (multinucleated cells, inclusions) are often a result of this CTL-mediated killing. (CTLs对于控制疱疹病毒感染至关重要，通过清除表达MHC I类分子呈递病毒抗原的感染细胞来实现。观察到的细胞病理效应(多核细胞、包涵体)通常是这种CTL介导的杀伤的结果。)

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) mediated by NK cells recognizing Fc receptors on infected keratinocytes.** ADCC involves antibodies binding to target cells, followed by NK cells (or other Fc receptor-bearing cells) binding to the Fc portion of the antibody and releasing cytotoxic granules. While antibodies might be present, the primary mechanism for eliminating virally infected cells presenting antigens via MHC class I is CTL killing, not ADCC. NK cells primarily target cells lacking MHC class I (missing self) or expressing stress ligands, not cells actively presenting viral antigens via MHC I. (抗体依赖性细胞介导的细胞毒性(ADCC)涉及抗体与靶细胞结合，然后NK细胞(或其他Fc受体携带细胞)结合抗体的Fc部分并释放细胞毒性颗粒。虽然抗体可能存在，但通过MHC I类分子呈递抗原的病毒感染细胞的主要清除机制是CTL杀伤，而不是ADCC。NK细胞主要靶向缺乏MHC I类分子(缺失自我)或表达应激配体的细胞，而不是主动通过MHC I类分子呈递抗原的细胞。)
- (B) **Complement-mediated lysis of infected keratinocytes due to deposition of C3b on their surface.** Complement activation can lead to cell lysis (via the membrane attack complex, MAC) or opsonization (via C3b). However, complement is generally less effective against intracellular pathogens like viruses, as the virus replicates inside the cell, protected from complement. Furthermore, CTL killing is the primary mechanism for eliminating infected cells presenting viral antigens via MHC I. (补体激活可能导致细胞裂解(通过膜攻击复合物(MAC))或调理作用(通过C3b)。然而，补体对细胞内病原体(如病毒)的效力通常较低，因为病毒在细胞内复制，受补体保护。此外，CTL杀伤是通过MHC I类分子呈递病毒抗原的感染细胞的主要机制。)
- (D) **Macrophage phagocytosis of infected keratinocytes opsonized by antibodies and complement.** Macrophages are phagocytes that engulf pathogens and cellular debris. While they play a role in clearing apoptotic cells resulting from CTL killing, they are not the primary mechanism for directly killing virally infected cells presenting antigens via MHC class I. Opsonization by antibodies and complement can enhance phagocytosis, but this is not the main cytotoxic mechanism against virus-infected cells in this context. (巨噬细胞是吞噬细胞，吞噬病原体和细胞碎片。虽然它们在清除CTL杀伤产生的凋亡细胞中起作用，但它们不是直接杀死通过MHC I类分子呈递抗原的病毒感染细胞的主要机制。抗体和补体介导的调理作用可以增强吞噬作用，但这并不是在此背景下针对病毒感染细胞的主要细胞毒性机制。)
- (E) **Neutrophil-mediated extracellular trap (NET) formation surrounding infected keratinocytes, leading to tissue damage.** NETs are webs of DNA, histones, and antimicrobial proteins released by neutrophils. They trap and kill extracellular pathogens, particularly bacteria. NETs are not the primary mechanism for eliminating intracellular viral infections or infected cells presenting viral antigens via MHC class I. (中性粒细胞胞外诱捕(NET)是由中性粒细胞释放的DNA、组蛋白和抗菌蛋白网络。它们捕获并杀死细胞外病原体，特别是细菌。NETs不是消除细胞内病毒感染或呈递病毒抗原的感染细胞的主要机制。)

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Correct Answer (C) - CTL Killing | Distractor (A) - ADCC (NK cells) | Distractor (B) - Complement Lysis | Distractor (D) - Macrophage Phagocytosis | Distractor (E) - NET Formation |
| :---------------------- | :-------------------------------- | :------------------------------- | :-------------------------------- | :--------------------------------------- | :----------------------------- |
| **Target Recognition**   | MHC Class I + Viral Peptide       | Antibody (Fc) on target cell     | C3b deposition on target cell    | Opsonins (Ab, C3b) on target cell       | Extracellular pathogens        |
| **Effector Cell**       | CD8+ Cytotoxic T Lymphocyte (CTL) | NK cell                          | Complement components             | Macrophage                               | Neutrophil                     |
| **Mechanism**           | Perforin/Granzyme, Fas/FasL        | Cytotoxic granules release      | MAC formation                     | Phagocytosis                              | DNA/protein web formation      |
| **Primary Role in Virus** | Eliminating infected cells        | Killing Ab-coated cells          | Limited role against intracellular | Clearing debris/apoptotic cells          | Limited role against intracellular |
| **MHC Class I Relevance**| Essential for target recognition  | Not primary mechanism            | Not primary mechanism            | Not primary mechanism                    | Not primary mechanism          |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
CTLs are the primary mediators of cell-mediated immunity against intracellular pathogens, particularly viruses, by recognizing viral antigens presented on MHC class I molecules. This leads to the targeted killing of infected host cells. Understand the difference between CTL killing (MHC I dependent) and NK cell killing (MHC I independent or stress ligand dependent) or ADCC (antibody dependent). Differentiate CTL mechanisms from complement lysis, phagocytosis, and NET formation, which target different types of pathogens or cellular debris. (CTLs是针对细胞内病原体(特别是病毒)的细胞介导免疫的主要介质，通过识别MHC I类分子呈递的病毒抗原来实现。这导致了对感染宿主细胞的靶向杀伤。理解CTL杀伤(MHC I依赖性)与NK细胞杀伤(MHC I独立性或应激配体依赖性)或ADCC(抗体依赖性)之间的区别。区分CTL机制与补体裂解、吞噬作用和NET形成，它们靶向不同类型的病原体或细胞碎片。)
